# 12 — Decision Record

Every decision taken against `design_overview`, with its rationale. **Nothing here is open.**
When implementation forces a change, amend this file in the same PR and add a row to §9.

---

## 1. Dependencies

`design_overview` §8's versions are stale, and four of the named crates are unmaintained. The locked
set lives in `13-dependencies.md`; the substitutions and their reasons are:

| Spec | Decision | Reason |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `libmpv = "2.0"` | **`libmpv2` 6.0** | The `libmpv` crate is abandoned and targets a pre-0.35 mpv ABI that no current distribution ships. `libmpv2` implements the libmpv 2.0 client API (mpv ≥ 0.35) and is maintained. |
| `ratatui = "0.26"` | **0.30.2** | Four minor versions of API change. This choice sets the project's **edition 2024**; the actual MSRV is **1.97.1**, driven not by ratatui itself (which needs only 1.88) but by `notify-rust` 4.18 and `image`'s transitive `quantette`/`safe_arch`/`wide` (1.89–1.90) — see §9. |
| `crossterm = "0.27"` | **0.29, via `ratatui::crossterm` only** | Two crossterm versions in one binary produce two incompatible `Event` types. Never a direct dependency. |
| `ratatui-image = "1.0"` | **11.0.6** | Requires ratatui ^0.30.1 and image ^0.25.6 — compatible with the above. |
| `reqwest = "0.12"` | **0.13** | Current major. rustls only; no OpenSSL, which is the largest source of cross-compilation pain for the release targets. |
| `toml = "0.8"` | **1.1** | Reached 1.0. |
| `fuzzy-matcher = "0.3"` | **`nucleo-matcher` 0.3** | `fuzzy-matcher` last released in 2020. |
| `dirs = "5.0"` | **6.0** | Current; still maintained. |
| (unstated) | **`fs4`** over `fs2` | `fs2` last released in 2018. |
| (unstated) | **`jiff`** over `chrono`/`time` | Modern API with correct duration and timezone arithmetic. |

`design_overview` §8 also omitted roughly twenty crates the design actually requires. They are all
enumerated in `13-dependencies.md`; that file is the sole authority.

---

## 2. Ratings removed

`design_overview` §6 binds `1`–`5` to `SetRating`. **Emby has no numeric rating.**
`POST /Users/{UserId}/Items/{Id}/Rating` accepts exactly one parameter, a boolean `Likes`
(confirmed against the Emby REST reference, *UserLibraryService*).

Shipping a star control that silently does nothing is worse than not shipping it. The feature is
removed: no `SetRating` action, no star display, no keybinding. **Favourites (`f`) is the only
user-item signal.** `1`–`5` are left unbound.

Thumbs up/down against the real `Likes` field would be implementable, but it was not asked for and
is not in the design. It is not planned.

---

## 3. Features cut

| Feature | Decision | Reason |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| **Spectrum analyzer** | Removed | mpv exposes no cheap FFT tap to a client. The options were an RMS-driven fake labelled as a visualiser, or piping audio to a second decode path for a real FFT. The first is dishonest, the second costs real CPU for decoration. Removed from the Zen view, the config (`ui.show_spectrum_analyzer`), and all wireframes. |
| **Crossfade (0–10 s)** | Removed | mpv has no native crossfade. It requires a second mpv handle, a 20 Hz volume-ramp task, role swapping, and careful de-duplication of playback reporting — the single most complex piece of the audio work, for a feature that conflicts with the project's stated bit-perfect priority. **Gapless is the only transition mode.** `audio.crossfade_sec` is removed from the config. |
| **1–5 star ratings** | Removed | See §2. |

These are **cut, not deferred**. No task implements them and no doc references them.

---

## 4. Keybindings

`design_overview` §6 and §7 contained fourteen conflicts, disagreed with each other in four places,
and defined **no quit key**. The full replacement table is `04-state-and-input.md` §6 and is
normative.

### Approach
The default keymap is **flat**: one chord, one action, in every view. Conflicts are structurally
impossible rather than resolved by context-scoping — which also means help text and inline hints
never need context qualifiers. Two structural moves make it work:

- **Seek moved from `h`/`l` to `[`/`]`** (and `{`/`}` for ±30 s). The spec bound `h`/`l` to both
  column navigation and seeking. Vim navigation is preserved intact.
- **Selection toggle moved from `Space` to `.`**. The spec used `Space` for both play/pause and
  toggling a visual selection. `Space` is now unconditionally play/pause, which is what users
  expect from the more frequent action.

### Other resolutions
| Spec conflict | Resolution |
| :-- | :-- |
| `l` = column-right *and* `ToggleLyrics` | Lyrics moved to `L` |
| `h` = column-left *and* history sub-view | History moved to `H` |
| `p` = PlayPause (§6) *and* prev_track (§7) | `p` = **PrevTrack**; `Space` is the sole play/pause |
| `n` = InsertNext (§6) *and* next_track (§7) | `n` = **NextTrack**; insert-next is `i` |
| `g a`/`g l` (§6) *vs* `Ctrl+A`/`Ctrl+L` (§7) | `g`-prefixed sequences only; the keymap supports two-chord bindings |
| `q` = quality cycle, and **no quit key existed** | Quit is `Ctrl+Q` (and `Ctrl+C`); `q` keeps quality cycling |
| `d` = Download *and* RemoveFromPlaylist | `d` = download everywhere; removing an entry is `x`; deleting a playlist is `X` |
| `r` = ReplayGain, colliding with a retry affordance | Retry/refresh is `Ctrl+R` |
| No mute or repeat binding existed | `M` = mute, `R` = cycle repeat |
| `+`/`-` volume | `=` and `_` also accepted, since `+` needs Shift on most layouts |
| `Alt+1..9` intercepted by tmux and many terminals | `F1`–`F9` added as equal-status aliases |

Wireframe §3.1's footer "Press `[v]` or `[Space]` to toggle" becomes "`[v]` or `[.]`".

### User-config conflicts
Validation runs on **every config load**, not only in the editor. The **last binding wins** so the
app is always usable, and every conflict is reported three ways: a startup toast, a badge with
per-row detail in Settings → Keybindings, and a `WARN` log line naming the chord and all bound
actions. **Conflicts never abort startup.** The in-UI remapper refuses a conflicting capture and
highlights the incumbent. See `04-state-and-input.md` §7.

---

## 5. Configuration schema changes

**Removed:** `audio.crossfade_sec`, `ui.show_spectrum_analyzer` (features cut, §3).

**Added:**

| Key | Default | Reason |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| `schema_version` | `1` | Enables migrations; without it the first breaking change corrupts every user's config |
| `servers[].device_id` | generated UUID | Emby session identity must be stable across restarts, or the server accumulates orphan sessions |
| `cache.prefetch_on_play` | `true` | Controls the write-through caching policy in `06` §4 |
| `cache.prefetch_next` | `1` | How many upcoming queue tracks to read ahead into the rolling cache; see `06` §4 |
| `cache.image_cache_mb` | `200` | Artwork is budgeted separately from the track LRU |
| `ui.restore_autoplay` | `false` | See §6 |
| `ui.enable_websocket` | `true` | Lets users behind restrictive proxies disable it |
| `ui.ascii_only` | `false` | Fallback glyph set for terminals with poor font coverage |
| `ui.show_lyrics` | `true` | Lyrics pane visibility, persisted |
| `audio.replaygain_preamp_db` | `0.0` | Standard ReplayGain control the spec omitted |
| `[logging] level, max_files` | `info`, `5` | Log rotation |

### Token storage
The spec stores `access_token` in plaintext. Mitigations adopted now: `0600` file mode on Unix, a
startup warning if the file is group- or world-readable, and redaction from every `Debug`/`Display`
impl and log line. The token must never reach `session.json`, `loxia-player.log`, or a test fixture — a CI
check greps for it. An OS-keyring backend is **not planned for 0.1.0**.

---

## 6. Session restore is paused

`design_overview` §4.2 says the session is restored on launch. It is — the queue, position, tab,
volume, quality profile, and EQ all return — but playback resumes **paused**, showing
`⏸ resumed at 01:24`.

Auto-playing on launch seizes the audio device and startles the user, particularly when loxia is
started from a shell for an unrelated reason. `ui.restore_autoplay = true` opts in.

---

## 7. Emby API resolutions

Confirmed against the public Emby REST reference (2026-07-21) and, for the highest-risk claims,
against a live Emby 4.9.5.0 server in task `02-01` (§10 below — read that section too, since it
overturns one of these). `03-emby-api.md` is the contract; the notable corrections to the original
design are:

1. **The ALBUMS / APPEARS ON split.** ~~An earlier pass of this document claimed
   `AlbumArtistIds` does not exist on `/Users/{uid}/Items`, based on the public reference not
   listing it, and proposed one recursive track query per artist grouped client-side by `AlbumId`
   as a workaround.~~ **Superseded by §10 item 1: `AlbumArtistIds` is real and verified working
   live.** The algorithm in `03-emby-api.md` §4 uses it directly — two lightweight album-level
   queries (`AlbumArtistIds` for primary, `ArtistIds` for all, diffed by id) — which is simpler and
   cheaper than the workaround this entry originally described.
2. **Lyrics are subtitle streams.** Emby has no lyrics service. `.lrc` sidecars are exposed as
   `Subtitle`-type `MediaStreams` on the audio item's MediaSource and fetched via
   `GET /Items/{Id}/{MediaSourceId}/Subtitles/{Index}/Stream.{Format}` (*SubtitleService*). The
   `/Items/` route — not `/Videos/` — is the one that works for audio. This is the mechanism other
   third-party Emby clients use. Feature **retained**; contract in `03-emby-api.md` §7. Confirmed
   live against real synced and unsynced content — see §10 item 3.
3. **Playlist removal requires `PlaylistItemId`**, not `ItemId`. `Playlist` entries therefore carry
   a `PlaylistEntryId`; dropping it makes removal impossible without a refetch. Confirmed live —
   exact field name match.
4. **Stream selection goes through `POST /Items/{Id}/PlaybackInfo`** rather than guessing at
   container support, and it is also how `MediaSourceId` is captured for lyrics.
5. **Ratings** — see §2.
6. Ticks are 100-nanosecond units; the conversion lives in one property-tested helper.

Task `02-01` re-confirmed parameter-level details against the project's live Emby server; its full
findings, including the correction to item 1 above, are in §10.

---

## 8. Architectural choices not in the spec

1. **A six-crate workspace** rather than one crate — the compiler then enforces "no I/O in the state
   machine", and agents get non-overlapping ownership.
2. **Action / Effect / Event with a pure reducer** — every behavioural requirement becomes a unit
   test with no mocks, no network, and no audio hardware.
3. **`AppState` on one thread, no `Arc<Mutex<_>>`** — removes a class of deadlocks from a UI that
   must never freeze mid-render.
4. **`MockEngine`** — the default test suite and all UI work require no mpv installation.
5. **The reducer never claims playback.** `PlayerState` changes only on `Event::Audio`, so the UI
   cannot lie about what the engine is doing.
6. **`loxia-player --doctor`** — a diagnostics subcommand. Not in the spec; makes support and release QA
   tractable.
7. **Licence: GPL-3.0-or-later.** The spec covers mpv's LGPL obligations but never states loxia's
   own licence. mpv builds are frequently compiled with GPL components, so GPL-3.0 is
   unconditionally safe, matches ecosystem norms, and removes a per-release audit of the bundled
   DLL's build flags.

---

## 10. Findings from the live API audit (task 02-01)

Confirmed against a real Emby 4.9.5.0 server. Two of these overturn what earlier research (against
the public `dev.emby.media` reference alone) had concluded — the audit gate did exactly its job.

1. **`AlbumArtistIds` exists and works.** Contrary to `03-emby-api.md`'s original claim (based on
   the public API reference not listing it), `GET /Users/{uid}/Items?AlbumArtistIds={id}` genuinely
   filters to albums where that artist is the album artist — verified with a bogus id (returns 0,
   not the unfiltered total) and against a real artist with both primary and compilation albums
   (`AlbumArtistIds` → 2, `ArtistIds` → 6, a real 4-album Appears-On split). The discography
   algorithm in `03-emby-api.md` §4 is simplified to two album-level queries
   (`AlbumArtistIds` = primary, `ArtistIds` minus `AlbumArtistIds` by id = appears-on) instead of
   one recursive track-level query with client-side grouping — cheaper and simpler. `02-05` and
   `02-06` are rewritten accordingly; `02-05`'s "must never emit `AlbumArtistIds`" acceptance test
   is removed.
2. **`POST /Playlists` takes `Name`/`Ids`/`UserId`/`MediaType` as query parameters, not a JSON
   body.** A JSON body attempt failed with `500 Unrecognized Guid format`; the identical call as
   query parameters succeeded. `03-emby-api.md` §8 and task `02-08` corrected.
3. **Lyrics confirmed working exactly as documented**, including a detail worth calling out: the
   `Stream.{format}` extension must match the discovered stream's codec exactly (`.lrc` for an
   `lrc`-codec stream, `.srt` for `text`/`txt`) — requesting the wrong extension for a given stream
   returns an empty body with `200 OK`, not an error. A real `.lrc` stream produced genuine
   centisecond timestamps (`[00:08.66]`); a `text`-codec stream produced `.srt`-shaped content with
   every cue's timing zeroed (`00:00:00,000 --> 00:00:00,000`), confirming the "treat SRT-sourced
   lyrics as unsynced" design decision was correct even when the container nominally carries
   per-line timing.
4. **The CI fixture-secret scan's "32+ character hex string" heuristic is unusable against real
   Emby data.** Real responses are legitimately full of 32-character hex `Id`s, `Etag`s,
   `ImageTags`, and `PresentationUniqueKey`s — indistinguishable from a token by shape alone; the
   check failed on every fixture captured for this task. Narrowed to match only actual
   token-bearing field names (`AccessToken`, `access_token`, `api_key`, `X-Emby-Token`,
   `X-MediaBrowser-Token`) and `api_key=`/`token=` query parameters. `ci.yml` and task `00-04`
   corrected.
5. **`Overview` cannot be set on playlist creation.** Passing it as a creation query parameter
   produces no error but silently fails to persist (verified live: read-back is `null`). Setting a
   description requires a second call after creation — `GET` the full item DTO, set `Overview`,
   `POST /Items/{id}` with that DTO. `02-08`'s `create` no longer takes an `overview` parameter;
   `set_overview` is a separate function, and the save-playlist modal (`10-08`) calls it as a
   second step only when the Description field is non-empty.
6. Confirmed as documented, no changes needed: `Views` → `CollectionType: "music"`; `Artists` /
   `AlbumArtists`; album tracks via `ParentId` + `SortBy=ParentIndexNumber,IndexNumber`;
   `MusicGenres`; favourites via `Filters=IsFavorite`; playlist items carrying `PlaylistItemId`
   (confirmed as the exact field name); playlist item removal via `DELETE .../Items?EntryIds=`;
   search; `InstantMix`; `PlaybackInfo`; favourite toggle (`POST`/`DELETE FavoriteItems`); image
   fetch; the `embywebsocket` upgrade handshake.

### Per-row results (2026-07-22, against Emby 4.9.5.0 at the project's server)

| # | Check | Result |
| :-- | :-- | :-- |
| 1 | `AuthenticateByName` returns `AccessToken`/`User.Id` | ✅ |
| 2 | `Views` includes a `CollectionType == "music"` entry | ✅ (two music libraries present) |
| 3 | `Artists?ParentId&Recursive=true` | ✅ |
| 4 | `Artists/AlbumArtists?ParentId` | ✅ |
| 5 | Discography query/algorithm | ✏️ — see item 1 above; rewritten to the `AlbumArtistIds`/`ArtistIds` two-query diff |
| 6 | Album tracks via `ParentId`, disc/track sort | ✅ |
| 7 | `MusicGenres?ParentId` | ✅ |
| 8 | Favourites via `Filters=IsFavorite` | ✅ (0 results on this library; endpoint shape confirmed) |
| 9 | Playlist items + entry id field name | ✅ — field is exactly `PlaylistItemId` |
| 10 | Playlist item removal (`EntryIds`) | ✅ — verified with a throwaway playlist, created and fully cleaned up |
| 11 | Search per type | ✅ |
| 12 | Instant Mix | ✅ |
| 13 | `PlaybackInfo` — `MediaSources[].Id`, `MediaStreams` | ✅ |
| 14 | Lyrics: subtitle stream discovery + fetch | ✅ — see item 3 above for the format-matching detail |
| 15 | Track without lyrics | ✅ — no `Subtitle`-type stream present |
| 16 | Favourite toggle | ✅ — verified on/off, restored to original state |
| 17 | Image fetch | ✅ |
| 18 | WebSocket upgrade handshake | ✅ — `101 Switching Protocols` with a valid `Sec-WebSocket-Accept` |
| 19 | `AlbumArtistIds` rejected/ignored? | ✏️ — **it is neither rejected nor ignored; it works.** See item 1 |

Two additional findings outside the original 19-row list, found while exercising playlist creation
for row 10: `POST /Playlists` needs query parameters (item 2 above), and its `Overview` parameter
is silently ignored (item 5 above).

| 2026-07-21 | Task 00-02: `loxia-audio` granted a narrow exception to allow `tokio` (`sync` feature only) — `AudioBackend::subscribe()` returns a `tokio::sync::mpsc::UnboundedReceiver`. See `13-dependencies.md` rule 7. |
| 2026-07-21 | Task 00-02: MSRV raised from 1.88.0 to **1.97.1** (edition 2024 unchanged). `ratatui` only needed 1.88, but `notify-rust` 4.18 requires 1.89 and `image`'s transitive `quantette`/`safe_arch`/`wide` require 1.89–1.90 at the versions locked in `13-dependencies.md`. Downgrading those crates to dodge the MSRV would reintroduce the staleness this project is explicitly correcting, so the toolchain moved instead. Pinned to the exact current stable release for reproducibility, not a floating `stable` channel. |
| 2026-07-21 | Task 00-02: `ratatui-image` built with `default-features = false, features = ["crossterm"]`. Its defaults (`chafa-dyn`, `image-defaults`) pull in a system `libchafa` via pkg-config and enable `image`'s full format set. Verified against its `Cargo.toml`: `icy_sixel` and `image` (with the `png` feature) are unconditional dependencies regardless of these features, so Sixel/Kitty/iTerm2/half-block rendering — all native Rust — is unaffected. No system library dependency needed for album art. |
| 2026-07-21 | Task 00-05: dropped `notify-rust`'s `images` feature. It exists to embed raw pixel data (`Notification::image_data`), which task 10-10 never uses — it only ever calls the path-based `icon`/`image_path`. `images` forwards to `image/default-formats`, which via Cargo's feature unification enables `image`'s full format set project-wide (not just for notify-rust), pulling in `exr`/`ravif`/`rav1e`/`quantette` and, transitively, the **unmaintained** `paste` crate (RUSTSEC-2024-0436), which failed `cargo deny check advisories`. Removing the feature fixes the advisory failure and is strictly more correct, not a workaround. |
| 2026-07-21 | Task 00-05: `[bans].multiple-versions` set to `"warn"` globally rather than a hard `"deny"`, because cargo-deny's check is graph-wide and cannot single out specific crate names — a hard deny would fail CI on harmless transitive duplicates (`rand` via `ratatui-image` and `proptest`, `windows-targets` via `souvlaki` vs. `ratatui-image`) that are out of our control. The four crates that structurally must not duplicate (`tokio`, `reqwest`, `ratatui`, `image`) are enforced instead by a dedicated CI step (`cargo tree --duplicates`, matched only on unindented/column-0 lines — `cargo tree -i <name>` itself errors out rather than listing versions when a name is ambiguous, so that flag can't be used here). Likewise, `crossterm` **and `time`** are **not** in `[bans.deny]` — both are required transitively (`crossterm` via `ratatui-crossterm`; `time` via `ratatui-widgets` and our own `tracing-appender`), so banning either name outright fails the build even though both are genuinely superseded *for our own code*. "Never a direct dependency of a loxia crate" is enforced for both by grepping `crates/*/Cargo.toml` instead. |
| 2026-07-21 | Task 00-05: licence allow-list corrected against what `cargo deny check licenses` actually found: our own crates declare the SPDX identifier `GPL-3.0-or-later` (matching `Cargo.toml`'s `license.workspace = true`), not bare `GPL-3.0` — added the former. `CDLA-Permissive-2.0` added for `webpki-roots`' CA certificate bundle data (pulled transitively by `rustls-platform-verifier` and `tokio-tungstenite` for TLS root verification) — a data licence, not a code licence, and permissive by design. |
| 2026-07-21 | Task 01-02: moved "unparseable keybinding value is dropped with a warning" out of `config::validate()` and into `03-05`'s `KeyMap::from_config`. `validate()` runs before the keymap parser (`03-04`) exists in the crate's build-out, and reaching forward into a not-yet-built module is exactly the kind of forward dependency the task ordering is meant to prevent. `from_config` already parses each override to apply it, so handling a parse failure there is a one-line addition, not new scope. Keybinding *conflict* detection was already deferred to `03-05`/`03-07` for the same reason — this extends the same reasoning to parse failures. |
| 2026-07-21 | Task 01-02: `theme.rs` gains a minimal `BUILTIN_THEME_NAMES` constant (the 7 names, `docs/07-ui-spec.md` §12) ahead of the full `Theme` system in `04-01`, so config validation's "unknown theme falls back to `default_terminal`" rule has something real to check against instead of a hardcoded duplicate list that `04-01` would then have to reconcile with. `04-01` builds the rest of `theme.rs` around this constant unchanged. Same pattern for `config::schema::FACTORY_EQ_PRESET_NAMES` (the 8 factory EQ preset names, `docs/05-audio-engine.md` §5) — needed now for the custom-preset name-collision rule, reused by `09-03` when it embeds `assets/eq_presets.toml` instead of redefining the list. |

Added to the workspace's `reqwest` feature list; `13-dependencies.md` updated. Every query-string-building endpoint in `loxia-emby` needs this, so it is not scoped to one crate feature. |

| 2026-07-22 | Task 03-05: `03-01`'s placeholder `KeyMap { bindings: HashMap<(InputContext, KeyBinding), ActionId> }` is replaced by `03-05`'s authoritative shape, `{ bindings: BTreeMap<KeyBinding, ActionId>, hints: BTreeMap<ActionId, String> }` — no per-context dimension at all, since `Normal` is a genuinely flat table (`docs/04-state-and-input.md` §5) and `TextInput`/`Modal` are handled entirely inside `resolve()`, not stored per-context. Added a private `insertions: Vec<(KeyBinding, ActionId)>` log beyond the two given fields: a `BTreeMap` alone only remembers the current winner for a key, so without a log `validate()` has no way to see what a later insert silently overwrote — needed to make `default_keymap_has_no_conflicts` (and conflict detection generally) actually mean something. Caught a real bug this way: the first `hints` implementation used `entry(action).or_insert(hint)` on every insert, so a user override's new binding never displaced the *default's* hint (`user_override_replaces_default` failed on the first run, asserting `"space"` instead of the overridden `"ctrl+alt+p"`). Fixed by making `hints` a cache fully recomputed from the insertion log after all inserts, keyed off "is this insertion's binding still the one `bindings` resolves to" — and by having a user override remove the action's *previous* binding(s) before inserting the new one, rather than merely adding an alias. `resolve()`'s `Modal(kind)` handling only implements the three universal keys (`Esc`/`?`/`Ctrl+C`); no per-modal table exists yet since none of the individual modal tasks (phase 07/09/11) have defined one — anything else in a modal context resolves to `None` for now, a scope gap for those tasks to close, not a silent wrong answer (unrecognised keys are swallowed, never misrouted to the `Normal` table). |

(2) The *non*-autoplay (paused) case is worse: it leaves `player.current` set with nothing ever loaded into mpv, but the ordinary `PlayerAction::PlayPause` handler (`Space`) only ever sends a bare toggle — before this task, `current.is_some()` always implied `load_current` had already run in the same breath, an invariant session restore was the first thing ever to break. Fixed with one new mechanism serving both: `reducer::queue::resume_after_restore` (a `load_current` sibling that preserves `player.position` instead of resetting it to zero, since this is a resume, not a fresh play) and a new `PlayerState.restored_unloaded` flag, set by `restore` whenever it leaves a track current-but-unloaded. The autoplay branch calls `resume_after_restore` immediately; the paused branch defers it to the first `PlayerAction::PlayPause`, which checks the flag before falling back to its ordinary toggle. |

Fixed by redacting values while keeping header *names* visible (useful for debugging, no secret in a name) — caught only because building diagnostics-redaction tests for this task meant actually reading what the existing `Debug` impl did, rather than assuming rule 5 ("never log a token... redact in Debug/Display") was already fully satisfied. |

It also works over SSH, unlike an X11/Wayland clipboard API, which is exactly the environment a terminal music client is most likely to be copying a bug report from. | 2026-08-01 | **Field-reported bug, fixed**: typing into any Settings sub-editor's own text field (server-profile add/edit form, sort-profile rename/new-name buffer, EQ-preset save/rename buffer) did nothing at all, and the form then appeared to hang — only `Ctrl+C` (quit the whole app) still worked. Root cause: `crates/loxia-player/src/input.rs`'s `context_for` correctly entered `InputContext::TextInput` off any of four conditions (the flat `SettingsState.editing`, or the three sub-editors' own nested text/name buffers, added incrementally across `11-01`/`11-03`/`11-04`/`11-05`), but the *dispatcher* for that context, `text_input_action`, only ever checked the first of the four — every keystroke for the other three fell through to `filter_text_input_action` (the inline column-filter handler), a silent no-op with no active Miller column in the Settings tab to filter. Worse, `Esc` in that handler maps to `NavAction::Cancel`, equally inert here (no modal, no active column), so there was no way to back out of the "frozen" form short of quitting the whole process. Caught by neither `11-03`'s, `11-04`'s, nor `11-05`'s own test suites, because every one of their text-editing tests calls `SettingsAction::TextInput`/`CommitTextEdit` directly against the reducer (which was always correct) — the only test exercising the server editor through the real input path (`server_editor_list_keys_map_to_actions`) covered the list view's own single-letter commands, never the add/edit form's actual typing. Fixed by factoring both `context_for`'s check and `text_input_action`'s dispatch guard into one shared `settings_text_edit_active` predicate, so the two structurally cannot drift apart again; added regression tests (`typing_into_server_draft_field_reaches_the_reducer`, `_sort_profile_name_buffer_`, `_eq_preset_name_buffer_`) that go through `to_action` itself, not the reducer, to close exactly the gap that let this ship. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Field-reported bug, fixed**: every Settings text buffer (the plain row editor, the server-profile draft field, and the sort-profile/EQ-preset name buffers) could only ever be edited by backspacing from the end — there was no cursor to move, so fixing a typo anywhere but the last character meant erasing everything after it. Root cause: all four were plain `Option<String>`, with `TextInput`/`TextBackspace` only ever `push`/`pop`-ing at the end. Also compounding the same underlying "which field, and what's actually being typed" confusion the user reported: the *plain* row editor (`SettingsState.editing`) was never even rendered — `render_row` read `get(&state.config)` (the still-committed value) unconditionally, so typing into e.g. "cache directory" showed no live feedback at all until `Enter` committed it; the two sub-editor forms did echo live text, but only ever with the cursor glued to the end (`sort`/`eq`'s own `format!("{buf}█")`), never at a real, movable insertion point. Fixed with one new type, `state::settings::TextEdit { text: String, cursor: usize }` (character-indexed, so it can never split a multi-byte codepoint) replacing all four `Option<String>` fields, five new `SettingsAction` variants (`TextDeleteForward`, `TextCursorLeft`/`Right`/`Home`/`End`) routed identically to the existing `TextInput`/`TextBackspace` (`Left`/`Right`/`Home`/`Delete` were already mapped all the way from crossterm through `to_chord`, just never consumed anywhere past that), and a shared `reducer::settings::active_text_buffer` helper so those five (plus the two pre-existing actions) only ever need one `if let Some(buf) = active_text_buffer(state)` instead of the same four-way branch repeated seven times. Rendering gained a `views::settings::cursor_aware_spans` helper (reverse-video single-cell cursor, falling back to a plain flat span when the combined text would need truncating on a narrow terminal) used by all four editing sites, and `render_row` now shows the live buffer instead of the stale committed value. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Feature request**: the server-profile add/edit form's single `url` field is split into three — `Protocol` (`[←→]`/`Enter` cycles `http`/`https`, never free text), `Host`, and `Port` (both ordinary text fields, reusing the same `TextEdit`/cursor machinery every other field in this form already uses). `ServerConfig.url` itself is untouched (still one string) — the split is purely a form-editing convenience; `reducer::settings::build_server_url(protocol, host, port)` composes it back (omitting `:port` entirely when empty, rather than guessing a default from the protocol — Emby's own default, 8096, isn't the HTTP/HTTPS default either way), and `split_server_url(url)` does the inverse when opening the *edit* form for an existing profile, so a server saved before this change round-trips losslessly. Both are hand-written (`split_once("://")`, `rsplit_once(':')`), not the `url` crate — `loxia-core` cannot depend on it (or `reqwest`, which is where `loxia_emby::client::parse_base_url` already does real URL parsing, on the other side of the same dependency boundary), and Emby base URLs are never more than scheme + host + an optional port, well short of needing a real RFC 3986 parser. Known, accepted gap: a bracketed IPv6 literal host (`[::1]:8096`) would have `rsplit_once(':')` split inside the brackets rather than before them — nobody using this feature is expected to run Emby on a raw IPv6 address in practice. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Field-reported bug, fixed, severe**: adding the very first server profile ever (no server configured yet, so `bootstrap::connect` fails and `Workers::spawn_stubs` is what's actually running — exactly first-run onboarding) left "Test connection"/"Save" stuck showing "testing…" forever, with no way to proceed. Root cause: `spawn_stubs`' own "network" channel used the generic `spawn_stub` (`03-08`'s original scaffolding), which logs whatever effect it receives at `debug` and drops it — including `Effect::Net(NetEffect::TestServerConnection)`, which `11-03` deliberately designed to need neither an active `client` nor `library` (it builds its own ad-hoc `EmbyClient` from the raw candidate url/headers), specifically so it could work before the app has ever connected to anything. Nobody had actually exercised this path against a stub-worker `AppState` before — every existing test either drove the reducer directly or spun up the *real* network worker (which already, correctly, handles this effect) — so the gap between "the effect doesn't need a real connection" and "the worker that's actually running when there isn't one still drops it anyway" went uncaught. Fixed with a dedicated `spawn_network_stub` used only for the network channel, which handles `TestServerConnection` for real (calling the same `network::test_server_connection` helper the real worker uses, now `pub(crate)`) and falls back to the ordinary log-and-drop stub for everything else. `NetEffect::Reconnect` (the connectivity prober) has the same "doesn't strictly need a connection" shape and is *not* given the same treatment here — nothing depends on it firing before the first successful connect, unlike `TestServerConnection`, which is the entire mechanism by which that first connect ever happens. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Field-reported observability gap, fixed**: a "Test connection" failure the classifier can't put a specific name to (any HTTP status outside 401/403/404/429/5xx — a wrong port hitting some other service, a reverse proxy's own error page, a missing webroot path prefix) surfaced only as the bare phrase "the server reported an error," with genuinely nothing else anywhere — not on screen, not in the log file — to debug it with: `workers::network::test_server_connection` mapped every `EmbyError` straight to `.to_string()` with no `tracing` call at all on the failure path. Fixed two ways: `EmbyError::Server`'s own `Display` now includes the HTTP status code (never a secret, often immediately actionable on its own — e.g. 400 vs. a proxy's own 5xx), and `test_server_connection` now logs the full classified error (`Debug`, which still carries the response body) at `warn` on every failure path, truncated to 500 bytes so a proxy's own full HTML error page doesn't flood the log file. The response body itself still never reaches the screen (arbitrarily large/ugly) — only the log file, at `~/.local/state/loxia-player/loxia-player.log.<date>` on Linux (`Paths::log_dir`), readable at the default `info` level since `warn!` outranks it. |
| 2026-08-01 | **Field-reported bug, fixed, severe** — the very failure the logging fix above was added to diagnose: against a real server, "Test connection"/"Save" failed with HTTP 400, `"Value cannot be null. (Parameter 'appName')"`. Root cause: `auth::authenticate` (`POST /Users/AuthenticateByName`, the one-time login request a *candidate* server profile needs before any access token exists) sent only the profile's own custom reverse-proxy headers — never the mandatory `X-Emby-Authorization` client-identification header (`Client=`/`Device=`/`DeviceId=`/`Version=`/`Token=`) every other request in this codebase already carries via `client::build_headers`. Emby rejects a login request missing it outright with exactly this 400. Invisible to every existing test because `wiremock`'s own matchers never asserted header *presence*, only method/path — the very first time this path was ever exercised against a real Emby server (no live-server verification had been done anywhere in this project before now) is what surfaced it. Fixed by factoring the identification-header value out of `client::build_headers` into a shared `client::identification_header_value`/`build_identification_header` (usable with an empty, pre-login `Token=""`, which Emby accepts fine — only the *separate* `x-emby-token` header stays conditionally absent), and threading the draft's own already-stamped `device_id` through `NetEffect::TestServerConnection` → `workers::network::test_server_connection` → `auth::test_connection`/`auth::authenticate`. This is the one code path in the whole app that had never been exercised against a real server at all: every *already-saved* profile's connection (`bootstrap::connect`/`EmbyClient::new`) goes straight to `validate_token` against an existing token via the already-correct `build_headers`, and never calls `authenticate` a second time — so this bug blocked every single first-time "add a server" attempt, but never an already-configured one. |

This means the task's own premise for `AudioError::LibraryNotFound` ("libmpv2 fails at load time when libmpv is absent... catch it") is not something any Rust code in this process can ever observe: if libmpv is genuinely missing, the OS refuses to start the `loxia-player` process before `main()` runs, let alone reaches `MpvEngine::new()`. The closest failure this crate genuinely *can* catch is `libmpv2::Error::VersionMismatch` (an incompatible-but-present libmpv, since `mpv_client_api_version()` is a real runtime call that can report a mismatch) — mapped to `AudioError::LibraryNotFound` since the user-facing fix is identical ("install a compatible mpv"), with every other `libmpv2::Error` mapped to `AudioError::Init`. `missing_library_returns_helpful_error`'s literal scenario ("point the loader at a bad path") cannot be simulated for the same reason — doing so would prevent the *test binary itself* from starting, not just the `Mpv::new()` call inside it — so it isn't included; `map_init_error`'s `VersionMismatch` branch is exercised by inspection/type-checking instead (there is no test-only way to construct a fake `libmpv2::Error` version mismatch from outside the crate that would be meaningful, since the version numbers come from a real linked library at a fixed build). |

Added `crates/loxia-audio/build.rs` (`pkg-config` as a build-dependency) emitting both `cargo:rustc-link-search` and an rpath (`-Wl,-rpath,...`) when `pkg-config` finds mpv outside the default path — a no-op on a standard system-package install, where pkg-config either isn't needed or resolves to the already-standard path. This is infrastructure, not a workaround: it makes the crate build and run correctly on more systems, not just this one. |

Reusing `is_queueable` for preloading would have let an `Unavailable` entry preload while online, which can never actually be buffered regardless of connectivity. Fixed by computing an *implied* gap instead: `wall_clock_delay_until_the_first_low_position_event - that_event's_own_reported_position`, which backs out the throttle's own delay and leaves only the actual gap between track A ending and track B starting. More significantly, `af-command <label> change <args>` against a live, `lavfi`-wrapped `anequalizer` returned `MPV_ERROR_COMMAND` in every spelling tried (bare label, `lavfi`-wrapped label, inner `@label` filter-instance names, `"all"` as target) — this build's `ffmpeg -filters` shows no "command support" flag on `anequalizer` at all. Meanwhile, resetting the *whole* `af` property directly, mid-playback, measurably did **not** restart the track or reset `time-pos` (0.289s → 0.301s immediately after → 0.627s 300ms later, monotonic throughout) — the opposite of this task's own stated assumption ("rebuilding the af chain per keypress makes mpv restart playback"). Design reshaped accordingly: `mpv::filters::apply`/`clear` always do a full `af` property reinstall; `eq::band_command`/`af-command` are kept as pure, tested, spec-shaped functions (this task's own given signature) but are **not called** by the real engine path, documented in both files' own doc comments. |

| 2026-07-28 | Task 09-05: extended this task's own given `SleepTimer` shape with two fields it doesn't name — `armed_entry: Option<QueueEntryId>` and `pre_fade_volume: Option<u8>`. `armed_entry` (recorded at arm time, `reducer::modal`'s own `Modal::SleepTimer` submit handling) is what makes `EndOfTrack` "fire on the entry that was current when armed" rather than "whatever happens to be current when the next `TrackEnded` arrives" — without it, skipping to a different track and letting *that* one end naturally would also satisfy a naive same-shaped check. `pre_fade_volume` is what "restores the original volume after stopping" needs: `state.player.volume` is an engine-confirmed mirror the reducer's own prior `SetVolume` requests will eventually overwrite (rule 6), so by the time the timer fires, the pre-ramp volume is no longer recoverable from that field alone — it has to be captured once, lazily, the first tick the ramp actually begins. |

Confirmed structurally, not just by inspection: a manual skip (`PlayerAction::Next`/`Prev`) never synthesizes a `TrackEnded` action in this reducer at all (`queue::next`/`prev` call `load_current` directly) — so "skipping to another track does not fire it early" holds by construction, and `armed_entry`'s identity check (above) is what stops a *different* track's own later natural end from firing a timer armed for the one that was skipped away from. |
| 2026-07-28 | Task 09-05: `EndOfQueue`'s firing condition (`queue.position + 1 >= play_order.len() && queue.repeat == RepeatMode::Off`) deliberately excludes `Repeat::One`, not just the `Repeat::All` this task's own text calls out — `queue::advance_on_track_ended`'s own existing branches special-case `Repeat::One` to *always* reload the same entry regardless of position, meaning the queue never truly "ends" under it either. `Repeat::All` is separately prevented from ever reaching this check at all, since arming `EndOfQueue` already forces it to `Off` at arm time. |
| 2026-07-28 | Task 09-05: the fade-out ramp's window length is `min(10s, total_span)`, where `total_span` is the whole `Duration` sleep length for that trigger, or the whole track's own `state.player.duration` for `EndOfTrack`/`EndOfQueue` — not a fixed 10s denominator regardless of how much time is actually available. This is what makes "a track shorter than 10s ramps over whatever time remains" come out right: a 6-second track's ramp starts at full volume from position 0 (`remaining/window = 6/6 = 1.0`) and reaches zero exactly at the natural end (`0/6`), rather than starting already reduced to 60% of volume (`6/10`) the instant playback begins, which a fixed-10s denominator would otherwise produce. | 2026-07-28 | Task 10-04: three new `NavAction` variants were added because no existing action expresses mouse-specific semantics — `FocusColumnAt { column, index }` sets a column's cursor to an absolute index *and* moves focus there in one step (every keyboard action does at most one of those: `MoveUp`/`MoveDown` are relative deltas on the already-focused column only); `ScrollColumn { column, delta }` moves a specific column's cursor *without* touching `nav.focus` at all, since "scrolling targets the column under the pointer, not the focused one" (this task's own spec) would otherwise silently steal keyboard focus away from wherever the user was working just because their trackpad passed over an unrelated column; `FocusQueueEntry(QueueEntryId)` moves the Now Playing view's own cursor to an entry without jumping playback there, reserving that for `QueueAction::JumpTo` on an explicit double-click. All three are handled in `reducer::nav` (`focus_column_at`/`scroll_column`/`focus_queue_entry`), added to the `arb_nav_action` proptest generator, and each given its own missing-column/missing-id no-op test alongside the happy path. |

Every existing cursor-setter (`move_cursor`, used by all keyboard navigation) already enforces "section headers are never landed on" by walking `Column::selectable_indices()` — a click, unlike a keypress, can name *any* on-screen row including a header's, and `nav_right`'s own `unreachable!("section headers are never selectable")` means a subsequent double-click-to-drill-in on a cursor parked on a header would panic. Fixed with a new `nearest_selectable` helper: snaps the clamped target index to the nearest selectable index at-or-before it (or the first selectable index if none precede it), mirroring `move_cursor`'s own invariant rather than inventing a second one. Caught by the fuzzer specifically because `arb_nav_action` was extended (same task) to generate `FocusColumnAt` with arbitrary indices — a good example of a generated action variant immediately paying for the cost of adding it. |

| 2026-07-28 | Task 10-05: found and closed a real, load-bearing gap in keyboard input for modals — `keymap::resolve`'s own `InputContext::Modal(kind)` branch only ever resolved the three universal keys (`Esc`/`?`/`Ctrl+C`) plus `Modal::Help`'s own `j`/`k`/`Ctrl+U`/`Ctrl+D` scroll table (`10-03`); `Enter` had never been wired to `ModalAction::Submit` for any modal except the text-editing special case (`modal_text_input_action`, gated on `modal_focus_is_text`, which is `false` for `DevicePicker`). Also added `loxia_core::action::AudioEvent::DeviceUnavailable { id }` as a *specific* wire-level event, rather than folding this failure into the generic `EngineError(String)` like every other engine error: `AudioError::DeviceUnavailable`'s own `Display` is a fixed, generic sentence that never interpolates the device id (`loxia-audio::error`'s own "never interpolate a field's actual content" rule), so the id the picker's "could not switch to `<name>`" toast needs would otherwise already be gone by the time it reaches the reducer. `workers::audio::to_action` special-cases this one `AudioError` variant ahead of the generic fallback. `reducer::player::apply_audio`'s new `DeviceUnavailable` arm looks the device's human name up in `known_devices` (falling back to the bare id if it's since dropped out of the last enumeration), reverts `config.audio.*` to the pending swap's own `previous_*` fields only if the failure's `id` matches what's actually pending (an unrelated stale entry — there can be at most one — is left alone), and toasts `could not switch to <name>`. |

The device-picker modal's own "which device is currently active" bullet marker therefore reads `config.audio.device_id` (the last-*requested*, optimistically-written id) rather than `player.output.device_id` (the last-*confirmed* one) — the only source of truth that is actually populated in practice. Wiring a real confirmation event is a substantial, cross-cutting change (a new wire event, a new mpv property read, and auditing every other reader of `output.*`) out of proportion to "device picker modal," and is not named in this task's own acceptance criteria; flagged here for whichever future task actually wires mpv's own device-change notifications. |

| 2026-07-28 | Task 10-06: found and fixed a real, significant defect while implementing "Enter commits to config" — `reducer::modal::submit`'s `Modal::Equalizer` arm emitted `Effect::Audio(SetEq(draft_gains))` and `WriteConfig(state.config.clone())`, but never actually wrote `draft_gains` into `player.eq` (or anywhere else in `AppState`) at all. `build_equalizer_modal` reads `player.eq.gains` as the starting point for the *next* time the modal opens, so confirming an edit and reopening the modal showed the *pre*-edit gains, even though the engine was, by then, already playing the *post*-edit ones — the reducer's own state silently drifting out of sync with reality, invisible until the modal was reopened. Fixed by writing `player.eq.{gains,bypassed,enabled}` directly on `Submit`, the same "this is a setting, not an engine-confirmed fact" exception `PlayerAction::SetEqGain`/`SetEqPreset`/`ToggleEqBypass` already rely on (this module's own top-of-file doc comment) — and by updating `config.equalizer.{active_preset,enabled}` too, but only when `preset_idx` names a real, known preset. When it doesn't (the user tweaked bars without landing on any known preset), there is no field in `EqConfig` to durably save arbitrary, unnamed gains into — the edit still takes full effect for the rest of this run via `player.eq.gains`, just not across a restart; this task's own spec names no "save as new preset" action, so inventing one to close that residual gap would be scope creep. |
| 2026-07-28 | Task 10-06: found and fixed a second, narrower defect in the same area — `build_equalizer_modal`'s `preset_idx` was computed by searching only `FACTORY_EQ_PRESET_NAMES` (the eight built-in names), never `player.known_presets` (the actual factory-*and*-custom merged list `p` cycles through, `09-03`). Opening the modal with a *custom* preset active always started `preset_idx` at `None`, even though the active preset was perfectly findable in `known_presets` — this task's own `p` cycling needs a correct starting index to advance from, which is what surfaced the bug. Fixed by searching `known_presets` by name instead. |

| 2026-07-28 | Task 10-08: found and fixed the single most significant defect of the whole session so far — `reducer::modal::allows` (the "while a modal is open it owns the keyboard" gate every action passes through first, `03-07`) was missing `Action::Data(_)` from its permitted set. Every async reply — network, cache, audio — was therefore silently discarded by `reducer::apply`'s very first line whenever *any* modal was open, before ever reaching `apply_data`. This was never exercised end-to-end by any prior test (every existing `DataAction` handler test dispatched straight against `reducer::nav`/`reducer::modal`, bypassing `reducer::apply` and its gate entirely) — in the real, running app it meant `10-05`'s device picker could never actually show its own enumerated device list (`DataAction::DevicesLoaded` arrives while `Modal::DevicePicker` is still open, by construction: that is the whole point of firing `Effect::Audio(EnumerateDevices)` on open), and this task's own "existing playlists loaded via `FetchColumn` on open" would have been equally impossible to satisfy — discovered only because this task's own `existing_target_hides_name_and_description` test kept failing with the target stuck on `New` no matter what. Fixed by adding `Action::Data(_)` to the allowed set (a reply is never a keystroke that could conflict with whatever the modal captures, so it was never a *correctness* consideration, only an oversight) and added a dedicated regression test, `data_actions_reach_the_reducer_while_a_modal_is_open`. |

"Save to playlist" was therefore completely non-functional at the network layer in the real app: the reducer built the right effects, `open_modal`'s own "closes immediately and toasts `saving...`" fired, and then nothing further ever happened — no HTTP request, no completion or failure toast, a silent no-op indistinguishable from success at a glance. | 2026-07-29 | Task 10-11: reading `souvlaki` 4.18's own `dbus` backend source (the default feature, and the one this workspace builds) found that `MediaControls::set_metadata`/`set_playback`/`set_volume` are not blocking D-Bus round trips at all — `attach` spawns one dedicated OS thread that owns the actual `dbus::Connection` and its event loop, and every subsequent call just posts a message to a plain `std::sync::mpsc::Sender` that thread already owns. Only `MediaControls::new` (never fails on this backend — it just stores two names) and `attach` (where `Connection::new_session()` actually runs, and where a headless system with no session bus fails) are the real, possibly-blocking calls, and only `attach`'s own one-time result is what `init_failure_is_non_fatal` exercises. This is why `workers::mpris`, unlike `workers::notify`, needs no rate limiting of its own: outbound updates are cheap sends already throttled at the source by how often the reducer's own three trigger points actually fire, and only the one-time `init` (not every `apply_metadata` call) is worth running on the blocking pool. |

Built `AppState::settings_write_debounce_until: Option<Timestamp>`, armed via `state.clock.checked_add(WRITE_DEBOUNCE)` (`WRITE_DEBOUNCE = 1s`) on every accepted edit and fired from `reducer::tick` via `settings::maybe_write_config`, mirroring `SearchState::debounce_until`'s existing shape exactly rather than inventing a new pattern. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-01: validation-driven edit rejection needed one real, UI-reachable case to test against. `cache.rolling_max_gb`'s existing `validate_rolling_max_gb` rule (`<= 0.0` resets to `5.0`) was reused for this: its Slider control's own `min: 0.0` lets a user legitimately drag the value down to exactly `0.0` through normal input, `config::validate` then silently corrects it back to `5.0`, and `try_apply` detects the correction (by re-reading the field through the same control's own `get` after validation) and rejects the edit with `state.settings.error = Some((row, message))` rather than silently accepting the corrected value — the user's typed input and the stored value must always agree, or the edit is refused outright. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-01: `SysEffect::SetMouseCapture(bool)` is a new effect variant handled directly in `runtime.rs`'s main loop, intercepted before the generic `dispatch::dispatch` worker fan-out — no existing worker channel can act on a terminal-level `crossterm` capability toggle, so this one effect is applied inline via `execute!(stdout, EnableMouseCapture)`/`DisableMouseCapture`, mirroring `terminal.rs`'s own existing setup pattern, with failures logged at `debug` and never treated as fatal. Desktop-notification and lyrics toggles needed no equivalent plumbing since both are already read fresh from `state.config` at point-of-use everywhere else in the codebase — only the mouse-capture terminal mode is a one-time-set external OS/terminal state that has to be actively re-toggled on change. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-01: the task's own spec names no dedicated key for switching sections (only "click a section in the list"). Bound `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` to `NextSection`/`PrevSection` as a judgment call — this only applies within `InputContext::Normal` while `Tab::Settings` is active, and the Settings-specific resolver falls back to the factored-out `normal_resolve` for every chord it doesn't claim, so the global tab-switch bindings (`Alt+1..9`, `F1..9`) and quit/refresh bindings still work unchanged from inside Settings. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-01: found and fixed a real fallthrough bug during this task's own input wiring, not present in any earlier task. `settings_enter_action` initially returned `Option<Action>`, with `None` for any row that has nothing to "enter" (`Toggle`/`Select`/`Slider`/`Number`) — but the caller wraps the whole `settings_row_action` result in `.or_else(|| normal_resolve(...))`, so a `None` from the Enter sub-case incorrectly fell through to the *global* keymap table, where bare `Enter` resolves to `QueueSelection`. Pressing Enter on, say, a Toggle row would have queued whatever was selected in the last-focused Miller column — nonsensical inside Settings. Fixed by making `settings_enter_action` return a plain `Action` unconditionally: `StartTextEdit` for `Text`, `ActivateRow` for `Action`, and the harmless idempotent `Action::Settings(SettingsAction::MoveRow(0))` for everything else, so Enter is always fully claimed within the Settings tab. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-01: while building the section list, noticed `reducer::modal::open_settings_sorting` (task 10-09) only switched to the Settings tab and left the section/cursor unset — 10-09 itself documented this as a gap pending a real section model, which now exists. | 2026-07-30 | Task 11-01: fixed a pre-existing writing-style violation noticed in passing — `reducer::player::replay_gain_toast_text` built `"ReplayGain: {mode} ..."` with a capitalized leading word, inconsistent with every other toast string in the codebase (which are lowercase-leading per the established style). | 2026-07-30 | Task 11-02: `reducer::modal::open_confirm` has existed since `03-07` behind `#[allow(dead_code)] // exercised by later phases` — nothing ever actually called it before this task's own "reset all keybindings, behind a Confirm" needed a real yes/no gate, making this the first real usage. Discovered in the same breath that `Modal::Confirm` itself had never gotten a real renderer either — `render::render_modal_placeholder`'s generic fallback box only ever showed the bare title `"Confirm"`, with no room for the caller-supplied `prompt` text at all. Built `crates/loxia-tui/src/modals/confirm.rs` for real (prompt + `[Enter] confirm [Esc] cancel` footer) rather than let the first-ever confirmation dialog in the app show an empty box. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-02: with `Modal::Confirm` now rendered for real, every one of the 8 `ModalKind` variants has its own render function, which makes `render_modal_placeholder`'s old `if matches! { ... return; }` chain's trailing generic-box fallback unreachable code. Rewrote it as a `match modal { ... }` over all 8 variants instead (no fallback arm) — the compiler now refuses to build if a 9th modal kind is ever added without its own render call here, rather than silently drawing an empty titled box for it the way the old fallback would have. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-02: an unbound action needs to persist as something other than a real binding string — simply omitting the action's key from `config.keybindings` would leave its *default* binding in effect (`KeyMap::from_config` always starts from `KeyMap::defaults()`), not remove it. Added a literal `"none"` sentinel value, checked case-insensitively in `from_config` ahead of `parse_binding` (so it is a deliberate no-parse-attempted removal, not a caught parse error that falls back to the default with a warning). |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-02: found a real, load-bearing ordering bug while designing how a captured rebind gets applied. `config.keybindings` is a `BTreeMap<String, String>`, so `KeyMap::from_config` replays overrides in *alphabetical order by action name*, not in the order a user actually made the edits — rebuilding `state.keymap` from `config.keybindings` after every edit (the same way the original `03-07` `keymap_editor_submit` did) means that, given two or more overrides already on file, whichever action's name sorts alphabetically later always wins a shared key on every rebuild, regardless of which one was edited most recently. A second edit in an interactive multi-remap session could therefore silently un-remap an earlier one the instant a third, alphabetically-later override existed. Fixed by adding `KeyMap::rebind`/`unbind`, which mutate the live `bindings` map directly and incrementally instead of rebuilding from scratch — `config.keybindings` is still updated for what a future restart replays, but the *live* session now always reflects genuine edit order. A full fix for the on-disk replay order (e.g. switching `config.keybindings` to an order-preserving representation) is out of scope for this task and is called out here as an accepted, narrower gap: only a restart after several stacked edits in a specific pathological alphabetical relationship can still show the old symptom. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-02: `Ctrl+C` and `Esc` are enforced as reserved at two independent layers, deliberately. Structurally, neither chord can ever reach `Modal::KeymapEditor.captured` via the real input path — `Ctrl+C` is intercepted unconditionally before any context dispatch (`docs/04-state-and-input.md` §7's "a user must always be able to leave"), and a bare `Esc` while capturing is claimed by `CancelCapture` rather than ever becoming a captured chord. `keymap_editor_submit` still refuses both explicitly (`reserved_chord`) as defence in depth, since a reducer-level guarantee ("these two chords are never bindable") should not depend on exactly one caller behaving itself — the same standard `modal::allows`'s own gate applies to every other invariant in this file. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-02: the task's own spec names no dedicated keys for "reset row"/"unbind row"/"reset all" beyond the letters `d`/`x` and "a footer action" — bound `d`, `x`, and `R` (capital) respectively, scoped entirely to `InputContext::Modal(KeymapEditor)` while not `capturing` (a completely separate flat namespace from `Normal`'s own `d`/`x`/`R` — `ToggleDownload`/`RemoveEntry`/`CycleRepeat` — the same reuse-without-conflict precedent `Equalizer`'s `p`/`b` and `SortProfile`'s `Tab`/`e` already established). `d` (reset-to-default) is *not* gated behind the conflict-refusal flow a fresh capture goes through — a judgment call, since the task's own spec describes no confirmation UI for this specific key and `KeyMap::defaults()` is conflict-free by construction; the one narrow edge case (a *different* action was already rebound onto exactly this action's own default key earlier in the same session) simply displaces that other rebind's claim on it, the same as any other `commit_binding` call. |

| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-03: while researching this task, found that the actual scope goes well beyond a Settings sub-view — "switching servers... reconnect, and reseed the columns" requires rebuilding the network and cache workers at runtime, and no such mechanism existed anywhere: `workers::Workers::spawn`/`bootstrap::connect` were startup-only, called once from `main.rs`, with no effect/action path back into them. Closed this gap for real rather than stubbing it: added `Workers::reconnect` (replaces the network/cache senders in place, leaving audio/mpris/notify/events untouched, since a server switch has nothing to do with those), a new `SysEffect::ReconnectServer(ServerId)` handled directly in `runtime::run` (like `SetMouseCapture`, ahead of the generic per-worker dispatch, since rebuilding a client is `async` and needs `&mut Workers`/`&Paths`), and `bootstrap::open_cache` (factored out of `main`'s own startup sequence so both call sites share one implementation). `bootstrap::connect`/`seed_artists_column` turned out to already be written in a fully reusable way (plain `async fn(&mut AppState)`, never assuming it only runs once) — no changes needed there at all. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-03: a live switch's own reconnect reopens a *fresh* `RollingCache` over the same `cache_root` (via `bootstrap::open_cache`) rather than reusing the previous connection's `Arc<Mutex<RollingCache>>` — reusing it would require threading that handle through `runtime::run`/`run_terminal`'s own signature for the sole purpose of a rare, user-driven action. Accepted as a narrow, low-severity risk: the two `RollingCache` instances share a single, not per-server, on-disk manifest, so a brief overlap while the outgoing cache worker finishes queued work could let one instance's manifest write clobber the other's — at worst, stale LRU bookkeeping, never a lost or corrupted audio file, since the two instances' actual track/download paths are already disjoint by server id. |
| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-03: "persist the outgoing session under its own server id" needed a persistence layer that didn't exist — `Paths`/`loxia_cache::session` only ever had one fixed, unsuffixed `session.json`/`history.json`. Added `Paths::session_file_for(server)` and `session::save_for_server`/`load_for_server`, entirely additive: the existing unsuffixed file keeps its startup-restore role unchanged (still whatever was active at last quit), while the new server-scoped files are a second, independent persistence path used only by a live in-app switch (write on the way out, read back on the way back in to that same server later). While wiring this, moved the `SCHEMA_VERSION` constant's canonical definition from `loxia-cache::session` into `loxia-core::state::SESSION_SCHEMA_VERSION` (the latter re-exports it) — the reducer's own switch-confirmation code needs to stamp a fresh `SessionSnapshot` itself (a pure `AppState` copy, no I/O) and cannot depend on `loxia-cache` to borrow its constant, the same architecture-boundary reasoning `RESERVED_HEADER_NAMES` below hits from the other direction. |

| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-03: "Test connection" and "Save" are the exact same effect/reply pair (`NetEffect::TestServerConnection` -> `DataAction::ServerTestSucceeded`/`ServerTestFailed`), not two — `ServerDraft.saving: bool` marks which one a given in-flight request was, read back when the reply lands (`server_editor_test_succeeded`) to decide whether a success should *also* commit the draft into `config.servers`. This means "Save" always re-authenticates from scratch using whatever username/password the draft currently holds, even when only, say, a custom header changed — a direct, deliberate consequence of `user_id`/`access_token` never being typeable fields at all (this task's own spec: "invites users to paste one from another client, which produces a session that behaves strangely"), not an oversight; editing an *existing* profile's form always starts with blank username/password for exactly this reason. |

| 2026-07-30 | Task 11-03: the add/edit form has no "id" field — `docs/02-data-model.md` §8 describes `servers[].id` as "config-assigned," assigned by loxia itself, not typed by the user, matching `access_token`/`user_id`/`device_id`'s own "derived, not typed" treatment. A new profile's id is slugified from its name (`generate_server_id`, disambiguated with a `_2`/`_3`/... suffix on collision) at the moment `Save` actually succeeds, not at form-open time — a name typed and then changed before saving must not leave a stale, unreferenced id anywhere. |

| 2026-08-01 | Live-deployment bug report: `nav.rs`'s `items_loaded`/`discography_loaded` clamped a freshly loaded column's cursor with a bare `.min(len - 1)`, which can land it on a `MediaItem::SectionHeader` — the one item kind `selectable_indices()` itself excludes. This is what `drill_right`'s own `unreachable!("section headers are never selectable")` was guarding against, and a real user hit it (panic, app killed mid-session). Fixed at the source: both loaders now route the clamped index through the pre-existing `nearest_selectable` helper (already used by `focus_column_at` for mouse clicks, whose own doc comment incorrectly claimed every cursor-setter did this "by construction"). `drill_right`'s `unreachable!` arm was additionally hardened to a no-op `return Vec::new()` as defense-in-depth — a reducer must never panic regardless of how a `Column` was built. |

| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment bug report ("still stuck on yesterday's song, Enter does nothing"): the real root cause of both — `append_tracks_gated`/`insert_next` (`reducer/queue.rs`) only issued the engine-loading `load_current` when `queue.entries.is_empty()` beforehand. `session::restore` (`restore_autoplay` off, the default) leaves `queue.entries` non-empty but nothing actually loaded into mpv (`player.restored_unloaded = true`, meant to be cleared by the *next* `Space` press, `reducer::player::PlayPause`). Browsing to something new and pressing Enter therefore always saw `was_empty == false` and silently appended behind the restored queue forever — nothing ever got loaded unless the user happened to press Space first. Fixed by also triggering the load when `player.restored_unloaded` is true, landing `queue.position` on the first newly-queued track (`play_order.len() - queued`) rather than replaying the stale restored one, and clearing `restored_unloaded` — so selecting something new always plays *that*, whether or not a prior session's queue was still sitting there unloaded. Same fix applied to `insert_next` (`i`), landing on `insert_at` instead. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment bug report (pasted log: `unhandled effect: FetchColumn ... kind=Albums { of_artist: Some(...) }` / `Tracks { of_album: ... }`): `pagination_trigger` (`reducer/nav.rs`) fired for *every* `ColumnKind` once its `total` didn't fill a full 200-item page, with no awareness that several kinds are always loaded in one single-shot request and have no next-page concept at all — `Albums { of_artist: Some(_) }`/`Tracks { of_album }` (loaded via `FetchDiscography`/`FetchAlbumTracks`, `page_loaded` never advances past 0) and `Playlists`/`PlaylistTracks` (`07-03`'s own "no pagination on either" — `playlists::list`/`items` always fetch everything at once). For `Playlists` — which *does* have a `FetchColumn` handler, one that always re-fetches the *whole* list regardless of the page requested — the reply came back reporting `page: 1`, and `items_loaded`'s "page 0 replaces, page > 0 appends" rule then appended that same full list a second time onto the column already showing it. This is the confirmed root cause of the separately-reported, previously-unresolved "playlists are doubled": any playlist count under ~50 keeps the cursor within `PAGINATION_LOOKAHEAD` of the end almost immediately after loading, so the very next `j`/`k` press doubled the list. Fixed with a new `column_kind_paginates` gate restricting `pagination_trigger` to the kinds the worker's `FetchColumn` arm actually paginates (`Artists`, `Albums { of_artist: None }`, `Genres`, `GenreArtists`, `Folders`). |

Fixed by adding `loxia_emby::endpoints::items::albums` (`GET /Users/{uid}/Items?IncludeItemTypes=MusicAlbum&Recursive=true`, mirroring `artists()`) and a new `ColumnKind::Albums { of_artist: None }` arm in the network worker. | 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment bug report, with mpv logs pasted directly ("Cannot open file 'emby-track:22012': No such file or directory"): the real, final root cause of "music doesn't play at all" — confirmed, not the speculative `select_audio_backend`/`MockEngine` diagnostic added earlier this session (that toast never fired for this user; mpv itself was loaded and running, per these very logs). `load_current`/`resume_after_restore` always start playback with `placeholder_url`'s inert `emby-track:{id}` scheme — a deliberately non-resolvable placeholder documented as being "replaced almost instantly" by a corrective `Load` once `Effect::Cache(EnsureCached)` resolves. But `reducer::queue::cache_resolved` (the `CacheResolved` reply handler) only ever issued that corrective `Load` when the rolling cache already had a **complete local copy** (`path: Some(_)`) — the `path: None` case (cache miss, background download just starting) was treated as a no-op, on the documented-but-false assumption that "playback is already under way from whatever `Load` already started with." It never was: the placeholder is not a real URL, and `loxia_emby::stream::StreamUrl` — which builds the real, directly-streamable Emby URL (`api_key` embedded in the query string specifically so mpv can open it without needing `EmbyClient`'s header map, per that module's own doc comment) — was only ever used inside the cache worker's own background-download fetch (`run_fetch`), never handed to the audio engine. In effect, **no track could ever play until it had already been fully downloaded once** (confirmed by the pre-existing `second_play_makes_no_network_request` test's own name — the *second* play is what works). Fixed: `handle_ensure_cached` (`workers/cache.rs`) now always computes the real `StreamUrl` (cheap — pure string building, no I/O) and sends it as a new `stream_url: RedactedUrl` field on `DataAction::CacheResolved`; `cache_resolved` now always issues a corrective `Load`, using the local file on a hit and `stream_url` otherwise. `RedactedUrl` gained `Serialize`/`Deserialize` (needed since `DataAction` derives both) — its redaction is only about `Debug`/`Display`, not serialization, so this is safe. |

This was previously judged "working as designed" and the user was offered it as an opt-in enhancement rather than fixed outright; asked again, so implemented: `move_focused` now treats `NavFocus::Sidebar` (checked after `Search`/`NowPlaying`, which structurally sit on `Sidebar` too but already fully own `j`/`k` regardless of focus) as `prev_tab`/`next_tab` depending on the sign of `delta`. While tracing this, found `Tab::Favourites` has no cursor-movement wiring at all (`favourites.cursors` is only ever written by `favourites_loaded`'s own clamping and by tests) — a separate, real, but unreported gap, left unfixed and flagged to the user rather than silently patched, since favourites arrow-navigation was never asked for and its own fix (a `Favourites`-specific `move_focused` branch, the same shape `Search`/`NowPlaying` already have) is better scoped as its own change. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: "playlists are not being triggered for play" and "some playlists shown that don't have music files (video file playlists)" turned out to be the same root cause. `playlists::list` (`GET /Users/{uid}/Items?IncludeItemTypes=Playlist&Recursive=true`) had no `MediaTypes` filter — Emby's `Playlist` item type covers video/photo/book playlists too (confirmed by `playlists::create`'s own pre-existing `MediaType=Audio` query param, which is what narrows a *newly created* playlist to music; nothing narrowed the *list* the same way). Every non-music playlist on the server therefore appeared in the Playlists tab, and — since `playlists::items` converts every row to a `Track` via `.collect::<Result<Vec<_>, _>>()?` (the same "one bad row fails the whole batch" convention every other listing endpoint in this codebase already uses, e.g. `items::artists`/`genres`/`folder_children`) — queueing a non-music playlist failed outright the moment it hit a video entry, which read to the user as "nothing happens." Fixed by adding `ItemQuery::media_types` (a new `MediaTypes` query-param builder, the same shape `genres`/`artist_ids` already have) and passing `.media_types(&["Audio"])` in `playlists::list`. `playlists::items`'s own "fail the whole batch on one bad row" behaviour was deliberately left unchanged, matching that established convention — the fix is to stop putting non-music playlists in front of it at all, not to make it more forgiving. |
