Reworked `move_search_cursor` to treat the query line and all three result sections as **one flat list**: `↓` from the query steps into the first result, `↓`/`↑` flow across section boundaries, `↑` past the first result returns to the query line, and `↑` from the query line steps out to the tab sidebar. Also routed `↓`/`↑` from the query line (a `TextInput` context) to `MoveDown`/`MoveUp` in `input.rs`, scoped to the Search tab so the inline column filter — which deliberately keeps its column's own cursor — is unaffected. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: "up/down doesn't switch tabs on search/now playing" and "i need to hit left again to move across tabs." The sidebar-tab-switching added earlier only reached Miller tabs (which express "tab list focused" as `NavFocus::Sidebar`); `Search`/`NowPlaying` have no column stack to hang that off, so `move_focused` intercepted them for content nav before the sidebar check ever ran. Added `NavState.sidebar_focused` (`#[serde(default)]`, only consulted for those two tabs): `false` (resting, content-focused) on tab entry, flipped `true` by `←`/`h` (via the new `nav_left`) so `↑`/`↓` switch tabs, and back by `→`/`l` (via `nav_right`). New `switch_tab`/`park_on_sidebar` keep a run of arrow presses walking the whole tab bar rather than diving into the first tab with content (distinct from the `Tab`-key `next_tab`/`prev_tab`, which still cycle Search result *sections* — now gated on `!sidebar_focused`). `set_tab` resets `sidebar_focused` to `false` so an explicit `Alt+N`/click/`SetTab` jump focuses content; only arrow-cycling parks on the sidebar. The Search view stops highlighting a result section while `sidebar_focused`. Settings (its own two-level section/row dispatch, not routed through `NavAction`) is intentionally left for a follow-up. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: the inline `/` filter only matched rows *already paginated in*, so filtering a long Artists list for a band beyond the first 200 never found it. Added `load_next_page_for_filter`: applying/extending a filter on a paginated column pulls the next page, and `items_loaded` keeps pulling further pages while the filter stays active, so the whole list is progressively fetched and searched. Deliberately independent of `pagination_trigger` (which fires off cursor proximity, useless here since a filtered cursor never nears the end) and uses the *exact* completion test `(page_loaded + 1) * PAGE_SIZE >= total` rather than `pagination_trigger`'s looser one — with no cursor gate to absorb it, an off-by-one would fetch a guaranteed-empty extra page on every filter keystroke. Full client-side load is the accepted cost of client-side filtering; requests are deduped by the worker's `InFlightKey::Column` and gated by its semaphore, and matches appear progressively as pages arrive. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: "on settings ... i can't return to settings group." Settings has a two-level structure (a left section/group list, a right rows pane) but no way to step focus *back* to the group list, and — unlike every other tab — `←`/`h` there already means "decrease this row's value" (slider/toggle/dropdown), so it can't cleanly double as "go back." User chose `Esc` as the outward-step key. Added `SettingsState.section_list_focused` and three transitions (`FocusSectionList`/`FocusRows`/`LeaveToTabSidebar`): rows --`Esc`--> section list --`Esc`/`←`--> the main tab sidebar (`NavState.sidebar_focused`, so `↑`/`↓` switch tabs), and `Enter`/`→` step back inward. On the section list `↑`/`↓` pick a group; on the tab sidebar Settings' own dispatch returns `None` for `↑`/`↓` so they fall through to `normal_resolve` -> `move_focused` -> `switch_tab` (Settings sits on `NavFocus::Sidebar`, having no column stack). `set_tab` resets `section_list_focused` so every (re-)entry lands on the rows, matching other tabs' content-focused-on-entry default. The section-list border + selected-row highlight brighten when the list is the focused level and the content border brightens when the rows are — the only on-screen cue for the three levels (accepted the resulting settings snapshot border-colour churn). The existing `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` row-level section shortcut is kept unchanged as a faster alternative that skips the list. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: "not every song is played," still with `Cannot open file 'emby-track:...'` in the logs. Root cause was the inert placeholder reaching mpv. `load_current`/`resume_after_restore`/`cycle_quality` emit an `AudioEffect::Load` with `placeholder_url`'s `emby-track:{id}` (loxia-core can't build a real Emby URL) and rely on the cache worker's `CacheResolved` reply to follow up with the real one — but the placeholder still hit mpv first (an un-openable file → error), and worse, `preload_effects`' `AudioEffect::Preload` **appended** that placeholder to mpv's own playlist, so when the current track ended mpv auto-advanced straight into the broken entry: the skipped songs. Fixed in the audio worker (`workers::audio`): `is_placeholder_load` drops any `Load`/`Preload` still carrying the `emby-track:` scheme before it reaches the engine, so only real URLs (the `CacheResolved` corrective `Load`, or a `file://` cache hit) ever get there. Every real playback path (`load_current`/`resume_after_restore`/`cycle_quality`) already emits `EnsureCached` alongside, guaranteeing that corrective reply; `preload` alone has none, so gapless preloading is effectively disabled until a future task threads a real preload URL through the cache worker — an accepted regression, since the placeholder-based "gapless" never actually worked (it only ever queued an un-openable file). `placeholder_url` was made `pub` so the drop guard can assert scheme-parity with it (`placeholder_scheme_matches_core`). |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: the Now Playing Queue/History panes couldn't scroll — `render_queue_rows`/`render_history_rows` rendered from index 0 with a bare `.take(height)`, so once the queue outgrew one screenful everything past it (including the current track) was invisible, even as `↑`/`↓` moved an off-screen cursor over it. Added `scroll_start(len, cursor, height)` (centres the cursor, clamped at both ends) and render each pane from that offset. Stateless (recomputed each frame from `now_playing_cursor`) rather than a stored scroll offset, matching the panes' own already-stateless rendering. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: a server with several music libraries showed only one under Folders. `bootstrap::connect` only ever resolves `library[0]` and threads that single id everywhere, and the Folders root (`ColumnKind::Folders { of_parent: None }`) was hard-wired to that first library's own children. The root now lists **every** music library (`items::music_libraries`, mapped to `MediaItem::Folder`) as a top-level folder to drill into. `column_kind_paginates` was narrowed to `Folders { of_parent: Some(_) }` (a real directory's children paginate; the single-shot library list must not, or it would double like the Playlists column once did). | 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: the user expected `Enter` on a playlist/artist/album/song to **replace** the Now Playing queue and play, not append to it (which is what `QueueSelection` did). Confirmed with them: `Enter` replaces, `Shift+Enter` adds. Added `QueueAction::PlaySelection { full_context }`, implemented as "clear the queue, then run the normal `queue_selection` path" — the cleared queue is empty, so a track appends-and-plays immediately and a container's (album/artist/playlist/folder) fetch reply fills the empty queue and plays from the top, reusing the existing append-to-empty-starts-playback flow rather than a second play path. Guarded by `selection_is_queueable` so `Enter` on a non-queueing row (a bare genre, the Search query line) falls back to the plain `queue_selection` and never wipes a queue it wouldn't refill. Wired via `enter_action` (the `ActionId::QueueArtistOnly` binding — `enter` and its `a` alias); `Shift+Enter`/`A` (`QueueFullContext`) keep appending, unchanged. The `QueueArtistOnly`/`QueueFullContext` ActionId *names* are now legacy (kept to avoid breaking any serialized user keymap), so their human labels were retitled "Play (Replace Queue)" / "Add to Queue" in the help modal and inspector (snapshot label text updated). A container fetch that fails now leaves the queue empty — the accepted cost of replace over append. `a` follows `Enter` (both replace) rather than keeping its old "append artist-filtered" meaning; that filtered-append variant no longer has a dedicated key, an accepted minor loss. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: `Enter` on an album started playback from the *second* track (though the queue was built correctly from the first). Root cause was in the mpv translator, exposed by the new replace-and-play (`Enter` clears then reloads) combined with the placeholder-preload drop. `on_playlist_pos` emitted `TrackEnded { natural: true }` on *any* change of mpv's `playlist-pos` — its purpose was to detect a gapless advance across an appended playlist entry (`0 -> 1`). But mpv reports `playlist-pos = -1` whenever nothing is current, so a `Stop` (`0 -> -1`) followed by the corrective `loadfile` of the new first track (`-1 -> 0`) both counted as changes; the second one landed *after* the replacement queue had loaded at position 0 and advanced it to position 1 — the second song. Now that placeholder-append gapless is disabled (see the audio-worker drop above), mpv's playlist is always single-entry and `playlist-pos` only ever toggles `0`↔`-1` on stop/reload, so this signal was pure spurious-advance. Fixed by only treating a **forward move between two real (`>= 0`) entries** as a gapless advance; a single-entry track's genuine end is already covered by `on_end_file(EOF)`. Preserves correct gapless detection for if/when a real preload URL re-enables appended playback. |
| 2026-08-02 | Live-deployment follow-up: some tracks failed to play, occasionally with `http: Error reading HTTP response: Immediate exit requested` (mpv/ffmpeg) in the logs. Strongest hypothesis: `prefetch_on_play` (on by default). `EnsureCached` is only ever emitted for the track *currently* being loaded for playback, so its background download fetches the exact same bytes mpv is already streaming — a second request to Emby for the same item on the same device/session. Emby commonly drops the older stream when a newer one arrives on the same session, so the prefetch was killing the very playback it rode in on; it never helped the current play (mpv has its own network buffer, `OPT_CACHE`). Force-disabled at both `open_cache` call sites (`main`'s startup and `runtime::reconnect_server`) regardless of the config toggle, rather than gutting the cache worker's still-tested download machinery (`run_fetch`/`already_fetching`/`part_path`, kept for a future non-competing cache). A proper cache-while-playing wants mpv `--stream-record` (dump the stream mpv is already pulling) or prefetching the *next* track instead of the current — tracked for later. Also added diagnostic `tracing::info!` in the audio worker for every real `Load`/`Preload` handed to mpv (token-redacted URL + start offset) and a `debug!` for each dropped placeholder, so any residual "won't play" track can be traced to exactly what the engine was told to open. |
| 2026-08-03 | Live-deployment follow-up (search #1): navigating search results with `↑`/`↓` felt like there were "hidden options" between sections — the flat cursor moved through rows that weren't on screen. `sectioned_list::render_section` clipped each section to its (small, proportional) height with `.take(inner.height)` from index 0 and never scrolled, so once a section held more results than its few rows, the flat navigation walked the cursor onto clipped rows. | 2026-08-03 | Live-deployment follow-up (sort #1): the queue sort menu reordered but grouped wrong — "artist + year + album + track" scattered years and albums. Root cause: the `Year` rule keyed off each track's own `ProductionYear`, which is frequently missing or inconsistent per track; a `None` year sorts *last regardless of direction* (`compare_option_last`), so any track with a dirty/absent year tag was yanked away from its album-mates, splitting the album across the timeline. Added `sort::album_years` (per-album representative year = smallest non-`None` `ProductionYear` among the album's tracks) and `sort::compare_with_album_year`, and switched `apply_sort_profile` (the queue sort) to it — every `Year` rule now keys off the album's year, so an album sorts as one unit even with inconsistent track tags, and albums order chronologically as intended. | 2026-08-03 | Live-deployment bug: pausing for long enough killed playback — resuming afterwards played nothing. Classic network-stream pause behaviour: while paused, mpv stops reading the HTTP stream once its `cache-secs` buffer is full, so the connection idles and Emby (or a reverse proxy) eventually drops it; on resume mpv's read hits a dead socket and playback ends. Fixed by turning on ffmpeg's HTTP auto-reconnect via mpv's `stream-lavf-o` init option (`reconnect=1,reconnect_streamed=1,reconnect_delay_max=30`) — `reconnect_streamed` covers non-seekable transcode streams, the rest resume a seekable (Direct) stream from its byte offset. Set best-effort (`let _ =`, not `?`) so an older mpv/ffmpeg that rejects the option can't take audio down with it (a failed mpv init otherwise degrades to the silent mock engine). Only the long-supported reconnect options are used (dropped the newer `reconnect_on_network_error`, which an old ffmpeg would reject and thereby void the whole option string). |
| 2026-08-03 | Live-deployment bug + request: a user deleted all their sort profiles by accident (no confirmation) and then found "adding new doesn't work." Two fixes. (1) **Add works, but was invisible from empty:** the reducer add-flow (`SortEditorNew` -> type name -> `CommitTextEdit`) was correct, but `render_sort_editor` only drew the name-input buffer *attached to an existing profile's row* — with zero profiles the row loop is empty, so the input box never rendered and typing seemed to do nothing. Added a dedicated new-profile name-input row (rendered whenever `editor.creating`), suppressed the "no profiles configured" hint while creating, and scoped the in-loop buffer to renames (`!creating`) so a new name never overwrites an existing profile's row. (2) **Delete now confirms:** `SortEditorDeleteProfile` (`x`) opens a `Confirm` modal naming the profile (via the existing `modal::open_confirm`, mirroring `11-03`'s server-removal guard, superseding this action's original "no confirmation" note); the actual removal moved to `SortEditorDeleteProfileConfirmed`, dispatched by the modal's own `Submit`. |
| 2026-08-03 | Live-deployment request: a way to recover the built-in sort profiles after deleting them by accident. Added `SettingsAction::SortEditorRestoreDefaults` (bound to `R` in the sort-profile editor) which re-adds `SortingConfig::default()`'s two built-ins (`chronological_discog`/`release_chronology`), skipping any name that already exists so a user's own edits are never clobbered, repointing a cleared `default_queue_profile` at the built-in default, and toasting how many were restored. Surfaced in the editor footer (`[R] Restore defaults`) and the empty-state hint. |
| 2026-08-03 | Live-deployment batch (three). (1) **Sorting only worked unpaused:** `build_sort_profile_modal` defaulted the sort target to `Queue` only while `PlayStatus::Playing`, so pausing flipped it to `Column` and sorting "stopped working" on a paused queue. Now defaults to `Queue` whenever `queue.entries` is non-empty (paused or playing); `Tab` still toggles to `Column`. (2) **Replacing the queue kept the prior play/pause state:** mpv's `pause` property persists across `loadfile`, so a track loaded from a paused state stayed paused. The mpv `Load` command now clears pause after `loadfile` — loading a track is always "play it now" (the restore-paused path deliberately emits no `Load`, so it's unaffected). (3) **No Stop button:** added `TransportButton::Stop` (`⏹`, between play/pause and next) to the Now Playing transport controls, wired to `PlayerAction::Stop` (the same action `S` already triggers). |
| 2026-08-03 | Live-deployment request: a clickable Quit button in the sidebar, after Settings. Added a "Quit" row (`\u{23fb}`, or `x` in ascii-only themes) rendered directly beneath the nine tabs in `widgets::sidebar` — not part of `TAB_ORDER` (it switches no tab), with its own `HitTarget::QuitButton` that the mouse handler maps to `SystemEvent::Quit` (the same quit `Ctrl+Q`/`Ctrl+C` already trigger). Keyboard-focus/arrow navigation deliberately doesn't include it (it isn't a tab); it's a mouse affordance alongside the existing key bindings. No hint text (unlike the tabs' `Alt+N` digit) — it would read "unbound" whenever Quit isn't bound and clutters the 16-col sidebar. |
| 2026-08-04 | Follow-up to the sidebar Quit button: make it keyboard-reachable (the initial version was mouse-only). Added `NavState.sidebar_quit_focused` (`#[serde(default)]`, never persisted) — a virtual position at the end of the sidebar cycle. `switch_tab` now cycles `TAB_ORDER.len() + 1` positions: `↓` past the last tab lands on Quit, `↓` again wraps to the first tab, `↑` walks back; `set_tab` clears the flag so any explicit jump/click leaves it. `Enter`/`→`/`l` on the focused Quit row quit — intercepted at the top of `to_action` (ahead of the per-tab dispatch, so the Settings tab's own sidebar handler can't claim `Enter`/`→` first). The sidebar renders the Quit row with the focus marker/selection style when focused, and suppresses the active tab's own marker so only one row reads as focused at a time. |
| 2026-08-04 | Live-deployment bug + request, one shared root cause. `Ctrl+B` reported "no bit-perfect capable output available" on hardware that has one, while the Settings toggle happily enabled a bit-perfect that did nothing. (1) **Devices were never enumerated at startup:** the *only* emitter of `AudioEffect::EnumerateDevices` was the device-picker modal opening, so `player.known_devices` stayed empty until the user happened to press `O` — and `enable_bit_perfect` searches that list, so it always refused. `main` now fires `EnumerateDevices` once at startup. (2) **The two bit-perfect paths disagreed:** the Settings row was a plain `Control::Toggle` writing `config.audio.bit_perfect`, with no capability check, no forced EQ/ReplayGain-off/Direct, and no `SetBitPerfect` effect — so it "enabled" an inert flag. `adjust_value` now routes that row (matched by `BIT_PERFECT_LABEL`) through `reducer::player::toggle_bit_perfect` (the `Ctrl+B` implementation, made `pub(crate)`) and mirrors whatever it settled on back into the config, so a refusal can't leave the row showing "on". (3) **Output driver/device are dropdowns now:** both were free-text fields a user had to type an id into blind. `rows_for_section` gained a `devices: &[AudioDevice]` parameter (from `player.known_devices`) and builds `Control::Select` options as `"auto"` + detected drivers/ids, plus the currently-configured value if it is no longer detected (an unplugged device must not silently vanish from its own dropdown). Option strings are raw config values, not descriptions — `Control::Select` has no display-vs-value split, and the device picker (`O`) remains the rich-description surface. |
| 2026-08-04 | Live-deployment bugs (two). (1) **Stop did nothing:** `PlayerAction::Stop` fell through `reducer::player::apply_player`'s catch-all `_ => Vec::new()` arm — it was never implemented, so neither the `S` key nor the new player-bar Stop button worked. Added `stop()`: reports "Stopped" to Emby (before tearing down the status/position, the ordering `queue::clear` already relies on), sets `PlayStatus::Stopped`, rewinds `position` to zero, refreshes MPRIS, and emits `Effect::Audio(Stop)`. The queue is deliberately **kept** (that's what `QueueAction::Clear` is for). mpv's own `stop` unloads the file, so `restored_unloaded` is set — reusing the restored-but-unloaded flag means the next `PlayPause` issues a real `Load` at position zero rather than un-pausing nothing. (2) **Toast text was cut off:** the box was a fixed 40 cells (38 inner), so the 39-character "no bit-perfect capable output available" rendered as "…availabl". The box now sizes to its own longest visible message (min 40, capped at the available area), and the remaining truncation path uses `ellipsize` rather than `truncate` so a genuinely-too-narrow terminal shows a visible `…` instead of silently dropping the tail. |
| 2026-08-04 | Live-deployment follow-up: `Ctrl+B` still refused after devices were enumerated at startup. Root cause is upstream of loxia: mpv's `audio-device-list` mirrors alsa-lib's own namehints — the same set `aplay -L` prints — and on many machines that contains **no `hw:` PCM at all** (the reporting user's laptop lists only `hdmi:CARD=Generic,DEV=0..3`, `sysdefault:CARD=amdsoundwire`, `usbstream:…` and the PipeWire/Pulse plugins). `is_bitperfect_capable` correctly requires `alsa/hw:…` (anything else routes through ALSA's resampling `plug` layer), so nothing enumerated ever qualified even though the underlying cards are addressable directly as `hw:CARD=…,DEV=…`. Added `device::linux::raw_hardware_alias` — derives the `alsa/hw:CARD=…,DEV=…` companion from any enumerated ALSA PCM naming a card (`DEV=` defaults to `0`) — and `mpv::handle::with_raw_hardware_aliases`, which appends one per distinct card+device (deduped, described as "… (raw hardware)") to the enumerated list. Both the device picker and the Settings dropdown now offer real bit-perfect targets. Non-Linux platforms get a `None` stub: only ALSA hides raw devices behind namehint wrappers. Note this also repairs a capability the Settings dropdown had just removed — the field was free text before, so a user could type `alsa/hw:0,0` manually; now the option is enumerated instead. |
| 2026-08-04 | **Bit-perfect output removed entirely**, at the user's request. It was never verifiable on their own Linux machine (their `aplay -L` exposes no raw `hw:` PCM at all — only `hdmi:`/`sysdefault:`/`usbstream:` wrappers over PipeWire — and even the derived `alsa/hw:CARD=…` companion would have to win exclusive access away from PipeWire to prove anything), and the feature's correctness genuinely differs per OS (ALSA raw-device-vs-`plug` rules, WASAPI exclusive mode, CoreAudio HAL rate-switching), which is not something they can debug or support for Windows/macOS users. Shipping an audiophile feature that silently does nothing is worse than not shipping it. Removed: `PlayerAction::ToggleBitPerfect` + its `Ctrl+B` binding + `ActionId`; `AudioEffect`/`AudioCommand::SetBitPerfect`; `config.audio.bit_perfect`; `PlayerState.pre_bitperfect` + `DspSnapshot`; `OutputInfo.bit_perfect`; `AudioDevice.bit_perfect_capable` and the `[Bit-Perfect capable]` picker annotation; the Settings toggle row; the player-bar indicator; the equalizer's "DISABLED — bit-perfect mode" state and every `refuse_if_bit_perfect`/DSP-refusal guard (EQ, ReplayGain and quality cycling are now always available); and the `mpv` `audio-exclusive` handling. `device::raw_hardware_alias` went with it — it existed solely to make bit-perfect reachable. The whole per-OS `device/{linux,macos,windows}.rs` split existed *only* for bit-perfect capability detection and is deleted; `loxia-audio` now has no platform conditionals at all, and `cfg_blocks_confined_to_device_modules` stands as a guard against reintroducing one loosely. Device enumeration, hot-swap, and the Settings driver/device dropdowns are all unaffected. |
| 2026-08-05 | Live-deployment batch — one root cause behind three separate reports ("theme resets to default after restart", "equalizer doing nothing", "transcode seems to be doing nothing"). Several settings were written to `Config` when changed (and correctly persisted to disk) but only ever read back by the Settings UI: the **runtime** fields the rest of the app actually reads — `state.theme`, `player.quality_profile`, `player.replay_gain`, `player.eq` — were left at `Default::default()` on every launch, so the config was effectively write-only for them. Added `reducer::hydrate_from_config`, called from `main` immediately after `DataAction::PresetsLoaded` (resolving `equalizer.active_preset` into real gains needs `known_presets`), which applies all four and returns the `SetEq`/`SetReplayGain` effects that make them real in the engine. An unknown theme name leaves the current theme untouched rather than falling back silently. |
| 2026-08-05 | Live-deployment bug: the status bar rendered twice in Zen mode. `views::zen` draws its own two-line footer *inside the canvas*, while `render::draw` unconditionally also drew the global `player_bar` into `zones()`'s own `player` zone directly beneath it. Skipped the global bar while `state.zen_mode` is set — Zen owns its footer, matching `docs/07-ui-spec.md` §2's "Zen mode replaces the entire body". |
| 2026-08-06 | Live-deployment batch (three small fixes). (1) **Modals bled through:** a ratatui `Block` paints only its border, so every modal drew over — but did not erase — the view beneath it, leaving the canvas text visible inside the modal body (reported with the sort menu over Now Playing). All eight modals now `render_widget(Clear, modal_area)` before their block. A regression test renders the sort modal over a populated Now Playing and asserts no queue text appears right of the modal's own border; it fails without the `Clear`. (2) **Themes "didn't change the background":** widgets only colour the cells they write and many use `style::fg` (no background component at all), so every gap between them kept the terminal's default background. `render::draw` now paints the theme's own background across the whole frame first, as a base coat every widget composites onto. A theme whose `bg` is `Reset` (the default) still paints nothing, deliberately inheriting the terminal's. (3) **`ui.now_playing_format`:** the player bar's "what's playing" line was hardcoded as `{title} — {artist} ({album})`; it is now a user template (`model::format_now_playing`) supporting `{title} {artist} {album} {album_artist} {year} {track_number} {disc_number} {genre} {duration}`, editable from Settings → Interface. An unknown placeholder is left verbatim so a typo is visible rather than silently blanking the line; a placeholder with no data expands empty and the resulting `()`/`[]`/double-space debris is tidied, so `"{title} [{year}]"` on an untagged track reads `Motion`, not `Motion []`. |
| 2026-08-06 | Live-deployment bug: lyrics never appeared. `Track::try_from` hardcoded `lyric_stream: None` with a comment deferring discovery to "lazily, see 02-11" — but the only code that ever performed that discovery (`endpoints::lyrics::find_lyric_stream`, called from `endpoints::playback::playback_info`) is never invoked for a queued track. So `lyric_stream` was always `None`, `reducer::queue::load_current`'s `FetchLyrics` guard never fired, and the pane could never appear however `ui.show_lyrics` read. Fixed by discovering it during the DTO conversion itself, from the `MediaSources` the default `FieldSet` already requests — no extra request, and every path that builds a `Track` (queue, search, favourites, playlists, columns) gets it uniformly. |
| 2026-08-06 | Album art wired end-to-end at last, and the Now Playing / player-bar restructure the user asked for. `widgets::album_art` had been complete-but-dormant since `10-01` (its own doc comment said so): protocol detection, the encoded-`Protocol` LRU and `render` all existed, but nothing called them, `NetEffect::FetchImage` was never emitted by anything, and the worker **decoded each image and dropped it** — `DataAction::ImageLoaded` carries only `{id, tag}`. Closed the loop with an `ArtStore` (`Arc<Mutex<HashMap<ItemId, DynamicImage>>>`) written by the network worker and read by the render layer: decoded pixels deliberately do **not** travel through `Action`/`AppState` (a `DynamicImage` is render data; actions stay plain serialisable values), while the existing `ImageLoaded` reply still fires purely to trigger the redraw that picks them up. `render::draw` now takes an `Art` bundle (renderer + `&mut` cache + store) and *returns* effects, since a missing cover is only discovered at the moment art is drawn at a particular cell size; `runtime` owns the pipeline, detects the protocol once at startup, and dispatches whatever the render asks for. `ArtOff` gives every render test a one-line disabled bundle. Encoding happens in exactly one place (`render`, on a cache miss with pixels already in the store), so it costs at most once per (item, size). Restructure: the progress bar and transport controls left the Now Playing right pane — the transport now sits in the always-visible player bar beside the seek bar (`render_line2`), so it works from every tab rather than one, and is dropped automatically when the row is too narrow to hold it *and* a usable seek bar; the freed pane space goes to album art, sharing with the lyrics pane when lyrics are showing. |
| 2026-08-06 | Follow-ups to the art/restructure work, from live use. (1) **Zen's footer sat above three dead rows** — `zones()` still carved out the 3-row player zone in Zen even though `render::draw` no longer draws the global bar there; Zen's canvas now spans that zone too. (2) **Covers were postage-stamp-sized:** `art_rect` capped rows at a fixed per-context maximum (12/20) regardless of the pane, so a large terminal got the same small square. It now scales to whatever the pane offers (`rows = min(pane.height, pane.width / 2)`, preserving the ~1:2 cell aspect) and centres horizontally; `ArtSizeContext::max_rows` is gone, though the context still picks the HTTP fetch size. (3) **Real graphics protocols were never detected:** `detect_renderer` ran *after* `read_terminal_input` was spawned, and `Picker::from_query_stdio` writes a query escape and reads the terminal's reply off stdin — which the input reader had already claimed. Detection therefore timed out and fell back to halfblocks (chunky covers on Konsole) or produced nothing usable (Ghostty). Detection now happens in `run_terminal` *before* the reader is spawned, and the resolved renderer is passed into `run`. (4) The player bar gained a top border and one column of side padding so its controls aren't flush against the terminal edge; the player zone grew from 3 rows to 4 to keep all three content lines. |
| 2026-08-06 | Two follow-ups the user's logs settled definitively. (1) **Lyrics never fetched after a session restore:** the logs showed *no* `fetching lyrics` line at all, which ruled out the HTTP layer and pointed at the effect never being emitted. `resume_after_restore` — the path a restored-but-paused session takes when playback resumes — builds its own effect list and never included the lyrics fetch that `load_current` does, so a restored track requested nothing and the pane sat on "loading lyrics…" indefinitely with no request to explain it. Factored the decision into `lyrics_fetch_for(state, track)` (pane on, stream present, not already showing this track's lyrics) and called it from both paths. Note this also means the earlier `MediaSources`-based discovery *is* working — `audio_format` reads the same `media_streams` to populate the codec/bitrate readout, which the user can see. (2) **Covers looked small/blocky even after art scaled to the pane:** `ArtSizeContext::Inspector` requested `ImageSize::Thumb` (64px), which was right when the inspector pane was capped at 12 rows but badly wrong now that Now Playing's art fills the whole pane — a 64px asset upscaled over twenty rows. The fetch size is now chosen from the area actually being filled (`fetch_size_for(rows)`, `Large` above 8 rows), not from the calling context. |
| 2026-08-06 | Live-deployment follow-up: the logs now showed `fetching lyrics` **and** `lyrics fetched empty=false`, yet nothing rendered — which isolated the failure to the one step between them. `reducer::nav::lyrics_loaded` discarded any reply whose track wasn't the current entry, as a staleness guard. That guard is both redundant and a silent failure mode: `widgets::lyrics` already filters on the stored id, so a reply for a track the user has skipped past is ignored at render time anyway, while *any* mismatch here — however it arose — threw away a successful fetch and stranded the pane on "loading lyrics…" permanently, with a successful fetch in the logs and nothing on screen to match it. The reply is now stored unconditionally, keyed by its own track id. |
| 2026-08-06 | Live-deployment follow-up: the album art jumped and resized whenever lyrics were toggled, because `render_right_pane` sized the art from `lyrics::should_show` (which follows the `ui.show_lyrics` toggle). The art's share is now reserved from whether the current *track has* a lyric stream at all — a property of the track, not of the toggle — so toggling only fills or empties the area beneath the cover and leaves the art exactly where it is. A track with no lyrics still gives the whole pane to art. |
| 2026-08-06 | Zen had the same toggle-driven layout jump the Now Playing pane did, by a different route: `content_height` (which feeds `top_pad`, the vertical centring) was computed from `lyrics::should_show`, so toggling the pane re-centred the whole block and slid the cover up and down. Now derived from whether the current *track has* a lyric stream, matching the fix already applied to `views::now_playing`. A regression test asserts the art placeholder's own row is identical with the toggle on and off; it fails against the old expression. |
| 2026-08-06 | Lyrics still not rendering with a successful non-empty fetch in the logs: verified by direct render tests that both `Lyrics::Synced` and `Lyrics::Unsynced` draw correctly through the real view once `state.lyrics` holds them, and (previous entry) that the reply is now stored unconditionally — so the remaining unknown is the *content* Emby actually returns. `endpoints::lyrics::fetch` requests `Stream.lrc` for an `Lrc`-typed stream, and Emby's subtitle endpoint may not support `lrc` as an output extension at all (its documented conversions are srt/vtt/ass), in which case the body is whatever it chose to send and `parse_lrc` can yield lines that satisfy `is_empty() == false` while carrying no visible text. Extended the worker's log line to report the parsed variant, the line count and the first line's text so the next run identifies the payload rather than guessing at it. |
| 2026-08-06 | The extended log line settled the lyrics failure outright: `kind="unsynced" count=1 first=` — the server answered `Stream.lrc` with an **empty body**, which parsed into exactly one blank line. Two genuine bugs behind that one line. (1) `Lyrics::is_empty` tested only `Vec::is_empty`, so a single blank line counted as real content: `should_show` reserved the pane, `render` drew nothing, and the track looked like it had lyrics that never appeared. It now treats all-blank lines as empty. (2) `endpoints::lyrics::fetch` asked for `.lrc` because the stream's codec is `lrc`, but Emby's subtitle endpoint converts by extension and its documented outputs are `srt`/`vtt`/`ass` — `lrc` is evidently not among them, hence the empty body. `fetch` now tries the stream's own format first and retries once as `.srt` whenever the result is empty, splitting the single attempt into a reusable `fetch_as(ext, parse_as)`. Note `parse_srt` discards cue timings by design, so a track served this way shows plain unsynced lines rather than scrolling with playback — visible lyrics beat none, and a server that genuinely serves `.lrc` still gets the synced path with no second request. |
| 2026-08-06 | Zen's remaining toggle jump was in `render_detail`, which had two entirely different layouts selected by `show_lyrics`: a vertically-centred title/artist/album block when off, and a top-aligned one with a lyrics section beneath when on. Split the flag in two — `reserve_lyrics` (from whether the track *has* a lyric stream) chooses the layout, `show_lyrics` chooses only whether the `LYRICS` header and pane are actually drawn — so toggling fills or empties the reserved area without moving the text. The regression test now asserts both the cover's row and the title's row are identical with the toggle on and off, and fails against either old expression. Zen's footer also gained the same top border and one-column side padding the global player bar has (and `FOOTER_HEIGHT` grew 2 -> 3 to hold the border), so entering Zen no longer visibly restyles or resizes the bottom of the screen. |
| 2026-08-06 | `parse_srt` discarded each cue's `-->` line wholesale, so `.srt`-fallback lyrics were `Unsynced` and sat static while the track played. It now parses the cue **start** (`HH:MM:SS,mmm`, hours optional, `.` accepted alongside the spec's `,`) and emits `Lyrics::Synced`; the cue *end* is deliberately dropped, since a lyric line should stay lit until the next one starts rather than blanking out between cues. A multi-line cue becomes several lines sharing one timestamp, preserving the file's own line breaks. Degradation is two-tier: a cue whose timing won't parse contributes untimed lines (and its raw timecode line is still swallowed, never shown as lyrics), and a file with no parseable timing anywhere falls back to `Unsynced` exactly as before. Nothing downstream changed — `active_line` and `render_synced` already handled `Synced` fully. |
| 2026-08-06 | The scroll wheel worked in every Miller column but did nothing in Now Playing, because `scroll_action` only understood `HitTarget::ColumnItem` and that pane is not a column. Added `NavAction::ScrollNowPlaying`, which — like `ScrollColumn` — moves the pane under the pointer rather than whatever holds focus (a plain `MoveDown` would have switched tabs when focus was parked on the sidebar). A `HitTarget::NowPlayingList` backdrop is registered under the whole pane, *before* the entry rows so clicks still resolve to a row, giving the wheel a target in the History sub-view and in the blank tail below a short queue too. |
| 2026-08-06 | The Artists column read `0 albums` beside every artist: Emby's artist-list endpoint does not populate `ChildCount` for artist items, so `Artist::album_count` is always zero until a discography fetch fills it in. Rather than print a confidently wrong number, zero now means *unknown* — `Artist::counts_summary()` returns `None` for it and both the column subtitle and the inspector's counts line omit themselves. An artist with genuinely no albums cannot appear in a music library's artist list, so nothing real is lost. |
| 2026-08-06 | mpv re-reports the bitrate throughout a VBR track, and each change in digit count (`999` -> `1012`) shifted every field to its right — the `Output`/`EQ` readouts visibly danced once or twice a second. The bitrate is now right-aligned in a fixed four-character field, which covers every rate up to 9999 kbps; anything wider still pushes out rather than being truncated. |
| 2026-08-06 | `F1` was an alias for `Alt+1` (jump to tab 1) alongside `F2`…`F9`, so pressing the universal help key jumped tabs instead. `F1` now opens the help overlay next to `?`; tab 1 keeps `Alt+1` and gives up only its F-row alias, which is the smallest change that respects the convention. A mouse-reachable `[?]` button was added to the header immediately left of the clock — its label names the key that does the same thing, and its hit rect is computed in the same pass that lays out the header's right-hand cluster so the two cannot drift. It elides after the clock but before the server name. |
| 2026-08-06 | Zen's footer was three rows against the global player bar's four, so the bottom panel grew and shrank as Zen was toggled. `FOOTER_HEIGHT` now matches `layout::compute`'s `Constraint::Length(4)` exactly, and Zen's two rows are anchored to the *bottom* of that zone — leaving the blank row at the top, where the bar's title line would be — so the seek bar and the technical readout stay on the same two screen rows either side of the toggle. |
| 2026-08-06 | `g a`/`g l` only switched to the Artists/Albums tab, dropping the user on a plain alphabetical list with the playing artist still to be found by hand — the scope gap the 2026-07-25 entry left open. Both now build the same two-level stack a search drill-down does (`open_artist_stack`/`open_album_stack`, extracted and shared): the destination's own albums/tracks on top, the browsable root list seeded beneath so `←` still leads somewhere. A track crediting several artists goes to the first, which is the one the UI leads with everywhere else. Nothing playing is a no-op, with no tab switch — a half-action would be more confusing than none. |
| 2026-08-06 | Settings' quality, ReplayGain and equalizer rows wrote their config fields and stopped. Each has a *runtime mirror* on `state.player` that the rest of the app actually reads — the same fields `hydrate_from_config` establishes at startup — so the settings saved correctly and changed nothing until the next launch, while the identical keybindings (`q`/`r`/`e`) worked. `on_config_changed` now live-applies all three alongside theme and mouse. Quality reuses `cycle_quality`'s reload path (extracted as `reload_at_current_profile`), but skips the reload rather than refusing outright when the current track is local: `q` is an action on the playing track, whereas a Settings row is a *preference* and must stick regardless. Disabling the equalizer needs `SetEq(None)` to uninstall the chain, which `eq_effects` could never express (it returns no effect at all when disabled, safe only for its own callers, which never flip `enabled`) — hence `eq_curve`. |
| 2026-08-06 | Added `cache.prefetch_next`: read-ahead of the next N upcoming queue entries into the rolling cache, at the profile the current track is playing at. mpv's own `Preload` reaches one track ahead and keeps nothing on disk, so the cache only ever held what had already been played. It is one `CacheEffect::PrefetchAhead` carrying the whole run rather than N `EnsureCached`es, because that effect's worker owns a single in-flight slot and cancels whatever is running for a different key — N of them would cancel each other *and* the playing track's own fetch. The worker gives read-ahead its own slot: it cannot cancel playback's fetch or be cancelled by it, runs its tracks sequentially, and a new run supersedes the old one. It reads the manifest directly rather than through `resolve`, so a merely-read-ahead track does not touch `last_access` and outrank genuinely played entries in the LRU. **Default `0` (off)**: a background fetch alongside playback killed playback outright once before (2026-08-02 below); read-ahead only ever fetches tracks *other* than the one playing and should not trip that, but "should not" is not "verified against a live server". |
| 2026-08-06 | Added a tenth sidebar tab, `Album Artists`, between Artists and Albums, backed by `/Artists/AlbumArtists` (the `items::album_artists` endpoint existed and had never been wired to anything). `Artists` lists every performer credited on any track, which on a library with compilations or featured guests is a long list nobody organises by; the album-artist list is the one that matches how the library is actually filed. A separate `ColumnKind` rather than a flag on `Artists`, since pagination, in-flight dedup and the offline index all key off the kind — the offline index serves it from the same artist rows, the album-artist list being a strict subset of what the index holds. The tenth tab outruns the `Alt+1`..`9` digits, so `ActionId::JumpTab10` was added on `Alt+0`/`F10` (as a browser's tab bar does) and the `JumpTabN -> Tab` mapping shifted: 6 is now Album Artists, and Settings moved from 9 to 10. |
| 2026-08-06 | **Verified against the live server**, which corrected three things taken on inference. (1) `parse_srt` had a regression the fixtures could not show: Emby synthesises an `.srt` from a plain-text lyric sidecar — a third of the lyric streams in the library are `txt`/`text` — and every cue in it reads `00:00:00,000 --> 00:00:00,000`. Parsed as timing, `active_line` matches every line from the first frame and highlights the *last* one, so the pane sits at the bottom of the lyrics for the whole song, worse than not syncing. `has_usable_timing` now requires at least one non-zero stamp, and both parsers fall back to `Unsynced` without it. Confirmed on the real file: 37 lines, previously `active_line(0s) = Some(36)`, now `Unsynced`; a genuinely-timed `.lrc`-sourced `.srt` still syncs (first cue at 52.89s). (2) The `0 albums` subtitle was blamed on Emby not populating `ChildCount` for artists. It populates it fine — `FieldSet::default()` has always requested it — and the DTO conversion was hardcoding `track_count: 0` and discarding it. `ChildCount` counts **tracks**, not albums (an artist with 28 albums reports 289, and has 288 tracks), so the column now shows a real track count; the album count genuinely is not reported and stays unknown. The fixture, captured before the field was noticed, gained the counts the server really returns. (3) The 2026-08-02 "background fetch kills playback" hypothesis did not reproduce at all: holding a stream open while concurrently fetching another track never interrupted it, on the same device id, in both direct and transcode profiles, for the same item as well as a different one. `cache.prefetch_next` therefore defaults to `1`. One competing transcode returned a transient 500 that was not reproducible on retry — already handled, since a failed read-ahead is logged and stepped over. `prefetch_on_play` stays force-disabled regardless: it re-fetches the bytes mpv is already streaming, which is wasteful whether or not it is harmful. |
| 2026-08-07 | The Artists list wouldn't scroll past the first page **by mouse**. `scroll_column` moved the cursor and returned no effects at all, so the wheel walked to the end of the loaded page and stopped there for good, while `j`/`↓` (which route through `move_focused` -> `pagination_trigger`) paged normally. Confirmed the reducer was otherwise sound — a test walking a 2145-artist list one keypress at a time reaches the last artist — and confirmed against the live server that paging is not the server's fault either: `TotalRecordCount` stays at the library total across pages and `StartIndex` is honoured. `pagination_trigger` was split so the trigger can be aimed at an explicit depth (`pagination_trigger_at`), because the wheel targets the column under the pointer and deliberately never takes focus. |
| 2026-08-07 | Closed the multi-library gap flagged on 2026-08-02. `bootstrap::connect` resolved `music_libraries()[0]` and threaded that one id into every `ParentId`-scoped query, so Artists, Album Artists, Albums and Genres only ever showed the first library — measured live, a user's two libraries hold 2145/2531 artists and 103/991 album artists, and only the first set was reachable. The scope is now `Option<ItemId>`: several music libraries means `None`, which drops `ParentId` and lets Emby return the de-duplicated union (4486 artists, 1087 album artists — less than the sum, since artists present in both are returned once). Exactly one library stays explicitly scoped rather than becoming "the whole server", so a server that also holds non-music audio can't leak into the lists. |
| 2026-08-07 | The inspector's reserved art block (a bordered placeholder since `04-08`) now draws real artwork through `widgets::album_art`, so Artists and Album Artists show the artist's image and Albums its cover, above the metadata as asked. Its height is a third of the pane between a 6- and 14-row bound, rather than the old fixed 6: `fetch_size_for` already asks for the 600px asset above 8 rows, so a tall terminal gets a real image instead of an upscaled thumbnail, while the metadata beneath is never crowded out. Gated on `ui.show_inspector_art` (default on): off reserves no rows *and* issues no fetch, since the point of the switch is to spend nothing on artwork. Threading this required `inspector::render` — and `miller`/`folders`/`playlists` above it — to take `&mut Art` and return effects, the same shape `now_playing` and `zen` already had. |
| 2026-08-07 | `Enter` on an album the artist merely *appears on* queues only that artist's tracks, while `Shift+Enter` queues the whole compilation — the behaviour existed and was tested, but the inspector listed only `QueueArtistOnly`, labelled "Play (Replace Queue)", which says nothing about the filtering. So the choice was invisible unless you already knew it. Both actions are listed now, and on an `AppearsOn` row they are relabelled for what they actually do: "Play {Artist}'s tracks only" and "Add whole album". The artist's *name* comes from looking its id up in the Miller stack the user drilled through — `AlbumRelation::AppearsOn` carries only an `ItemId`, and the column that produced the album necessarily holds the artist. Elsewhere both keep their generic labels, since there is no filtering to explain. Also fixed `QueueFullContext` missing from the inspector's own label table, where the `_ => ""` fallback would have drawn the row blank. |
| 2026-08-07 | `Shift+Enter` did nothing at all, while the inspector advertised it as the key for "Add to Queue". Two causes, both fixed. First, the chord is **undeliverable**: distinguishing `Shift+Enter` from a bare `Enter` requires the terminal keyboard-enhancement protocol, which `terminal.rs` never pushes, so the terminal sends a plain `\r` (or `\n`) and the binding can never fire. `hint_for` returns an action's *first* binding, and `shift+enter` was listed before `A` — so the legend named the one key that could never work and hid the one that does. `A` is now listed first and is what the UI shows; `shift+enter` stays bound as an alias for terminals that do report it. Second, even where it fired it was invisible: appending to a queue that is already playing changes nothing on the current tab, so `append_tracks_gated` now toasts the count for that case. The play path stays silent — playback starting is its own feedback — and folders, which already announce their own count, are not told twice. |
| 2026-08-08 | The inspector showed a placeholder for almost every album but real art once you drilled into its tracks. Not a repaint bug (the worker sends `ImageLoaded` and the reducer `touch()`es), not the DTO dropping a field, and not the discography path synthesising albums — it fetches real `MusicAlbum` items through the same conversion. It was the data: an album usually has no image of its own, and Emby answers with an empty `ImageTags` plus `PrimaryImageItemId`/`PrimaryImageTag` naming the item that does. Measured live: of 300 albums 81 had their own tag, **195 had only the pointer**, 24 had nothing — against 287 of 300 tracks with their own tag, which is exactly why tracks looked fine. Fetching `/Images/Primary` untagged does not substitute: it 404s. Since the image can now live on a different item than the one being displayed, `image_tag: Option<String>` became `image: Option<ImageRef>` across `Artist`/`Album`/`Track`, carrying the holder's id with the tag so a URL can never be built for the wrong item. Verified by running real `/Items` responses through the real conversion: albums resolving to art went from 81 to 276 of 300. |
| 2026-08-09 | Two independent playback-control defects found together. **Seek jumped to the end of the track for every step size**: `SeekTarget::Relative` is signed *milliseconds* (its own doc comment says so, and the `[`/`]`/`{`/`}` bindings emit `5_000`/`30_000`), but `mpv::handle::apply_seek` passed the value to mpv's `seek` command, which takes *seconds* — so a 5-second step ran `seek 5000 relative`. The unit is converted at the boundary now; the test that covered this arm had asserted the buggy behaviour with a `Relative(-5)` fixture that no keybinding ever produces, and now uses the real emitted values. **Volume keys did nothing**: `PlayerAction::VolumeDelta` existed and `+`/`-` emitted it, but `apply_player` had no arm for it and it fell through to the `_ => Vec::new()` catch-all whose comment claimed only `SetDevice` still fell through. It now reads the engine-confirmed mirror to compute an absolute target and emits `SetVolume`, leaving the mirror to the engine's own `VolumeChanged` exactly as `ToggleMute` does. The player bar's technical line also gained a `Vol: nnn%` readout immediately before the EQ segment, right-aligned in a fixed field so a digit change doesn't shift the EQ text beside it, and reading `Vol: muted` when muted since the stored number stays whatever it was. Volume sits ahead of EQ in the collapse order, so a narrow bar now drops the EQ readout slightly sooner. |
| 2026-08-09 | The player bar's transport buttons responded unreliably to the mouse — Stop often did nothing or skipped a track, Play frequently did nothing at all — while the keyboard equivalents always worked. The four control glyphs `U+23EE`/`U+23EF`/`U+23F9`/`U+23ED` are ambiguous-width: `unicode-width` reports 1 cell, terminals draw them as 2-cell emoji. The hit map is built from our width, so each button's region drifted one column further left than its glyph: with a 2-space gap we registered Prev at 0, Play at 3, Stop at 6, Next at 9, while the terminal drew them at 0-1, 4-5, 8-9 and 12-13. Clicking Stop therefore hit column 9 — Next's region — or column 8, which belonged to nothing. Every glyph now carries `U+FE0F`, pinning emoji presentation so `unicode-width` and the terminal both say 2. The trailing gap was also folded into each button's own hit rect, so the strip has no dead columns between controls. The guarding test asserts each glyph measures 2 cells: every other assertion here would be circular, measuring the layout with the same `text::width` that built it. The same fix reaches Zen, whose footer reuses `render_line2`. |
| 2026-08-09 | Removed the player bar's `Output: <driver> (<device>)` segment, which always rendered empty. `PlayerState.output` was written by **nothing** in production code — the device picker persists `config.audio.{device_id,output_driver}` instead, and no path ever mirrored the engine's active device back into player state — so the field sat at `OutputInfo::default()` for the whole life of the process and the segment read `Output:  ()`. The dead `output` field and the `OutputInfo` type went with it rather than being left as a permanently-empty trap; nothing else referenced either, and neither appears in a session snapshot, so no on-disk format changed. A side effect worth noting: the technical line is shorter, so the EQ readout now survives at widths where it had started to elide. |
| 2026-08-09 | Every cached file was written as `.flac`. `CacheKey::filename` derived the extension from the quality profile alone — `Direct` -> `flac`, `TranscodeHigh` -> `mp3`, `Med` -> `opus`, `Low` -> `aac` — a mapping invented in `08-01` when the doc's example named a profile that no longer existed. Both halves are wrong: `Direct` streams the **source file untouched**, so the extension is whatever that file already is (mp3, opus, m4a, wav...), and a transcode profile's container comes from `transcode.target_codec`, the profile choosing only the bitrate. `layout::cache_extension(profile, source_codec, target)` now decides it, mirroring `loxia_emby::stream`'s own request-side mapping (duplicated across the crate boundary with the table tested on both sides, as `clamp_eq_gain` already does). `path_for`/`begin_fetch` take the extension from the caller since the key cannot know it; `resolve` never recomputes a path, so files cached under the old scheme keep playing until they are evicted. `begin_fetch` also now returns an existing row's stored path rather than recomputing, so a resumed fetch can't orphan its own `.part`. |
| 2026-08-09 | Added `PlayerState.playback_source`, surfaced in the player bar as `Source: cache` / `Source: stream`. `reducer::queue::cache_resolved` is the only place that learns whether a local copy existed — it already chose between a `file://` URL and the network one — so it records the answer there. `None` until that reply lands (at most the worker's own 50 ms resolve timeout) and again on every track change and stop: carrying the previous track's answer forward would assert something never checked, and the readout is simply omitted while unknown rather than guessing. |
| 2026-08-09 | `cache.prefetch_on_play` honours its config value again. It had been force-disabled at both `open_cache` call sites after a live user's playback died with `http: ... Immediate exit requested`, on the hypothesis that re-fetching the track mpv was already streaming killed the stream. That did not reproduce when measured on 2026-08-06: a concurrent fetch of the *same* item, on the same device id, in both direct and transcode profiles, never interrupted a held-open stream. It still costs double bandwidth on a first play — the copy only pays off on a later replay or offline — so the config default moved from `true` to `false` and it is now opt-in, which also makes the stored config match what the code actually does for the first time. |
| 2026-08-09 | The rolling cache was a flat `tracks/<server>/<item>.<profile>.<ext>`, so the directory was unreadable — nothing said which artists or albums had been fetched. It now mirrors the downloads tier: `tracks/<server>/<AlbumArtist>/<Album>/<disc>-<track> - <title>.<profile>.<ext>`, with the folder-naming helpers shared between the two so they cannot drift apart. Three consequences had to be handled. `reconcile` walked exactly two levels and skipped non-files, so it would never have seen anything beneath an album directory: every orphan and stale `.part` there would have accumulated untouched. It now walks recursively and prunes directories it empties, which eviction would otherwise leave as a permanent skeleton. Collisions became possible (the same album in two libraries), resolved with a ` (n)` suffix checked against the **manifest** rather than the filesystem — `layout::resolve_collision` short-circuits on a path that doesn't exist, which is exactly the state `begin_fetch` is in when it reserves a row before downloading. And since the manifest stores each row's path and `resolve` never recomputes one, entries written under the old flat scheme keep playing until they are evicted normally. |
| 2026-08-09 | Untimed lyrics could not be scrolled. Timed ones follow playback via `active_line`, but an unsynced set rendered from line zero with no offset at all (`render_unsynced`'s own comment admitted as much), so on a long track everything past the pane's height was unreachable. Added `AppState.lyrics_scroll` with `ActionId::LyricsScrollUp`/`Down` on `K`/`J` — the shifted pair of the `j`/`k` that move a list, both free in every context, so no chord gained a second meaning. The offset counts **source lines, not rendered rows**: the reducer has no idea how the pane wraps, and clamping against a guessed row count is what left `ASSUMED_VIEWPORT_ROWS` a standing wart, whereas skipping whole lines before wrapping makes the clamp exact and puts the scrolled-to line at the top of the pane. Applies to unsynced lyrics only — a manual offset on timed lyrics would just fight the auto-scroll — and resets whenever new lyrics arrive, so a track never opens mid-song or past the end of a shorter set. |
| 2026-08-09 | Lyrics rendered with their own timecodes as text — every line reading `00:00:00,000 --> 00:00:00,000` above the words. Two defects, found by fetching the exact track a user named (Amorphis, "The Gathering"). **The parser followed the stream's declared format, not the extension actually requested.** `fetch` asks for `.srt` for a `text`-codec stream (SRT is the form that can carry timings), but passed `r.format` — `Txt` — as the parser, so `parse_plain` read an SRT body and every cue number and timing line became a lyric line. The parser is now derived from the extension inside `fetch_as` itself, so the request and the reader cannot drift apart again. **And discovery took the first subtitle stream regardless of quality.** That track carries two: a `text` transcript at index 2 whose cues are all `00:00:00`, and an `lrc` stream at index 3 with the real times. `find_lyric_stream` now ranks `lrc` over `srt` over `text` and keeps file order within a rank. Verified against the live server through the real discovery and fetch path: the track resolves to stream 3 and 32 synced lines beginning at 28.06s, where it previously produced timecoded text. |
| 2026-08-09 | Renamed the shipped command and every on-disk location from `loxia` to **`loxia-player`**, at the user's request: `loxia` is a short enough word to collide with unrelated tools on `PATH`. The binary follows the renamed package (`crates/loxia-player`), and `paths::APP_DIR` — one constant, not three literals, because a half-renamed identity would read config from one directory and write cache to another and look exactly like data loss — moves config, cache, data and state to `loxia-player/`. Also renamed everywhere loxia identifies itself to something outside the process: the clap command name, the log file stem (`loxia-player.log`, matched by the retention sweep's prefix), the MPRIS `display_name`/`dbus_name` (hyphens are valid in a D-Bus *bus name* element, unlike an interface name), mpv's `audio-client-name` as it appears in the PipeWire/PulseAudio mixer, and Emby's `X-Emby-Authorization` `Client=` — which means the server records a **new device entry**, and the old one can be revoked. **No migration is performed**, deliberately: the user asked for the rename precisely to get a clean setup, so the previous `loxia/` directories are simply left where they are, to be kept or deleted by hand. The in-app header still reads `loxia`; it is branding rather than an identifier, and the longer name would cost seven columns of a width-constrained line. The rule applied throughout: anything another program, the OS or the server **keys on** — and any command a user types — became `loxia-player`; branding and prose (the header, the About title, the sleep-timer label, licence text, the `.loxia.json` download sidecar's own extension) stayed `loxia`. The first pass missed three things that only a non-source-file or a second look caught: the `justfile`'s `cargo run -p loxia` (it has no file extension, so the sweep skipped it — the user hit this immediately), the **reqwest `user_agent`** on every Emby API request, and mpv's own `user-agent` option. A rename is only done when the greps are run untruncated over `git ls-files`, not over a chosen extension list. |
| 2026-08-09 | A user reported that with two music libraries, album artists from both appeared but one library's albums never loaded and its folders were missing from the Folders root — then found it cleared after restarting the app. Diagnosed with `scripts/diagnose-libraries.py` against that server (the same Emby instance, reached through a Cloudflare Zero Trust hostname with service-token headers rather than on the LAN): the server side was entirely healthy — both libraries typed `music`, the union returning 4486 artists and 1087 album artists, and both sampled artists' `AlbumArtistIds`/`ArtistIds` resolving to real albums. So the fault was transient and local, and the shape of it points at one thing: `bootstrap::connect` resolves `library_scope` **once** per connection from a single `music_libraries()` call and holds it for the whole session. A partial or failed result there mis-scopes every browsing list until the next restart, with nothing on screen or in the log to say what was decided. That decision is now logged at `info` with the library names and the resulting scope, so a recurrence is diagnosable from the log rather than by inference. Deliberately not re-resolving the scope mid-session yet: one transient episode is thin evidence for a behavioural change, and the log will say whether it happens again. The diagnostic script itself gained two fixes on the way — raw `http.client` so header names go out verbatim (`urllib.request` capitalises them, turning `CF-Access-Client-Id` into `Cf-access-client-id`, which is what made it 403 against Cloudflare Access), and failure output that distinguishes a proxy rejection from an Emby one. |
| 2026-08-09 | Nothing played from a server reached through Cloudflare Zero Trust, while a second server was fine. The log named it: `youtube-dl failed: not found or not enough permissions` from `ytdl_hook`, then `Failed to recognize file format`. mpv issues its **own** HTTP request for the audio and does not share `EmbyClient`'s header map, so the proxy answered it with an HTML deny page — which mpv, unable to parse as media, handed to its youtube-dl hook. Every browsing request kept working, since those go through `reqwest` with the headers attached, which is exactly why this looked like a library problem rather than an auth one. `AudioEffect::Load`/`Preload` have carried a `headers` field since `05-05`, and `loxia-audio` has always applied it as a per-file `http-header-fields`, but **every one of the five construction sites passed an empty map** — the plumbing existed end to end with nothing ever put into it. `queue::stream_headers` now supplies the active server's custom headers, and returns empty for a `file://` cache hit or the inert `emby-track:` placeholder, neither of which is an HTTP request. Also set mpv's `ytdl=no`: this plays files from a media server, never a video site, and leaving the hook on means any unparseable response spawns a doomed youtube-dl subprocess whose failure buries the real cause. **Then a second defect surfaced behind the first**: with the headers finally attached, mpv answered `Expected '=' and a value.` and `Command loadfile: argument options can't be parsed`. `loadfile`'s options argument is *itself* a comma-separated `key=value` list, and `encode_headers` comma-joined the headers into it — so mpv read the second header as another option. A Cloudflare Access token is a client id **and** a secret, so it hit this every time, while any single-header server would have worked; the existing per-entry quoting never helped, because the separator was the problem, not the contents. The value is now wrapped in mpv's own `%n%` length-prefixed literal, the one form needing no escaping of commas, quotes or spaces. Verified against real mpv rather than reasoned about: `two_custom_headers_both_arrive` (an `mpv-tests` integration test with a local HTTP server asserting both headers arrive) fails on the old encoding and passes on the new. |
| 2026-08-09 | Five input and rendering defects reported together, four sharing a theme: a key or setting that was written down but never actually consulted. (1) **`Shift+Tab` did nothing anywhere.** Terminals send it as its own escape sequence, which crossterm reports as `KeyCode::BackTab` with **no** SHIFT modifier — never `Tab` with one. `to_chord` had no arm for it, so the chord was dropped before reaching any binding and every `Tab if mods.shift` arm in the codebase sat unreachable. (2) **Tabbing onto Settings trapped you there:** `settings_row_action` claimed `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` for section cycling, and since entering a tab focuses its content, there was no way to tab back out. Tab now means "next tab" everywhere without exception; sections are reached the way the left pane already provides (`Esc`, `j`/`k`, `Enter`). (3) **From the Search query line, `Alt+1`..`Alt+9` typed a bare digit into the query and `F1`..`F10` vanished.** The text-input arms match on `chord.code` alone, so a modified `Char` still read as text, and `to_action`'s `TextInput` branch had no fallback to the normal table — so anything it didn't claim was swallowed rather than passed on. Modified chords are no longer treated as text, and the branch falls through. (4) **The Playlists glyph `☰` (U+2630) is East Asian Wide** — a genuine two cells — so its label started a column right of every other tab's, in every theme except the two `ascii_only` ones, which never draw it. Replaced with `≡` (U+2261), and `sidebar_glyphs_are_one_cell_wide` now guards the whole set, the same shape as the transport-glyph guard. (5) **`ui.ascii_only` did nothing:** `Theme::ascii_only` is loaded from the *theme file* (only `green_crt` and `amber_crt` declare it) and the user's setting was never folded in — another config field written but never read, exactly the class `hydrate_from_config` exists to prevent. ORed with the theme's own value, so a vintage-terminal theme stays ASCII regardless, and re-derived on change so turning it off restores the theme's baseline. |
| 2026-08-09 | The Favourites heart and the Artists face rendered off-centre — the same width mismatch as the Playlists trigram, but in the opposite direction. `♥` (U+2665), `☺` (U+263A) and `⚙` (U+2699) are *text-default but emoji-capable*: `unicode-width` measures them as one cell while a terminal with an emoji font draws them as two, and since layout is computed from our number, the label beside them lands a column out. The same glyphs bite in three more places — the player-bar status marker (`▶`/`⏸`/`⏹`, which prefixes the whole now-playing line), the `⚠` availability marker, and the `⏱`/`⚠` header badges. `text::narrow_glyph` pins any such glyph with U+FE0E, the variation selector meaning "text, not emoji", and is applied at all four sites. It **detects** the case rather than hardcoding a list — if the emoji form would be wider, the glyph is at risk — so a glyph added later is handled without anyone remembering this exists; a glyph with no emoji presentation is returned untouched, so no terminal is asked to interpret a selector it doesn't participate in. ASCII is excluded outright: `#` and the digits only become emoji as part of a keycap *sequence*, which nothing here builds, and the guarding test caught the first implementation pinning `#` for nothing. Three width bugs of this family now (transport buttons, Playlists, these), so the checks are standing tests rather than one-off fixes. |
| 2026-08-09 | Filtering a long column could list the same row twice — reported as two "Ozzy Osbourne" entries under `/ozzy`, one of which vanished once scrolling pulled a further page, with only one present unfiltered. A page was being **applied twice**. Two paths independently request `page_loaded + 1`: `pagination_trigger` off cursor proximity and `load_next_page_for_filter` off an active filter, deliberately independent of each other so a filtered column keeps loading while the cursor sits still. The network worker coalesces concurrent duplicates by `InFlightKey::Column(kind, page)`, but `run_guarded` removes that key **before** sending the reply, so a request issued in the window between the fetch finishing and the reducer applying it escapes coalescing and fetches the same page again; `items_loaded` then appended it a second time. The duplicate disappearing on scroll was a later page-0 reply *replacing* the accumulated list. Fixed in the reducer rather than the worker, since it is the authority on what has been applied: a reply whose `page` is at or below `page_loaded` is dropped, only `page_loaded + 1` ever being requested. Page 0 stays exempt — it replaces rather than appends, which is how a refresh works. |
| 2026-08-09 | Two unrelated requests. **A genre now queues.** `queue_album_or_artist_row` refused a `MediaItem::Genre` with a toast, on the `07-04` reasoning that a genre can span tens of thousands of tracks so queueing one wholesale "is never the intent" — but a user selecting a genre is asking for exactly that, and said so. Added `NetEffect::FetchGenreTracksForQueue` + `items::genre_tracks` (filtered by genre **name**, as `genre_artists` already is, since Emby has no id filter for genres) and `QueueSource::Genre`, sorted album-artist → album → disc → track so a queued genre plays as coherent albums rather than an alphabetical run of titles. `selection_is_queueable` had to learn about genres too, or `Enter` would have refused to clear the queue for one. The size concern is handled by the append toast reporting the count rather than by refusing. Verified against the live server: 612 tracks for one genre, 61 for another, with that sort accepted. **Volume is restored to the engine, not just the mirror.** `session::restore` set `state.player.volume` from the snapshot, so the player bar showed the saved value, but nothing ever told mpv — which starts every run at its own default of 100. The number on screen was right and the sound was not. `restore` now emits `AudioEffect::SetVolume`, the same shape `hydrate_from_config` already uses for ReplayGain and the EQ, for the one setting that lives in the session rather than the config. |
| 2026-08-09 | Queued genres must follow the user's configured sort order, not one the code picks — and chasing that surfaced a pre-existing defect of the same shape on artists. Every non-folder queue fetch was re-sorted client-side by `(disc, track)` alone. For a single album that is exactly right; across albums it is nonsense, grouping every album's track 1 together, then every track 2 — and it silently undid the album-aware ordering the server had already applied to `artist_tracks`. `order_incoming` now splits the three real cases: a **folder** keeps the server's file-path order (unchanged); a source spanning albums (**genre**, **artist**) is ordered by the queue's active sort profile, falling back to the configured default and then to album order if neither resolves; anything else (an album, a playlist, a hand-picked selection) keeps disc/track, since `Enter` on an album means "play this album", not "apply my library sort to it". Uses `compare_with_album_year` rather than a bare `compare`, so a `Year` rule keys off each album's own year — the same correction `apply_sort_profile` already makes. The genre endpoint's own `SortBy` now only makes paging deterministic and no longer decides play order. Verified the artist test fails against the old behaviour, producing exactly the interleaved `["late one", "early one", "late two", "early two"]`. |
| 2026-08-09 | A sort could not be undone. `apply_sort_profile` reordered `queue.entries` **in place**, which destroyed the order the tracks were queued in — an album's disc/track order, a playlist's own order, a folder's file paths — so there was no way back to it short of re-queueing. Shuffle never had this problem, because it permutes `play_order` and leaves `entries` alone; sorting now works the same way, which makes "default order" a plain reset of `play_order` to the identity, exactly what `unshuffle` does. The sort menu gained a first row, `Default order (as queued)`, recognised by having **no rules** — which is also the only sensible reading of a user-created empty profile, so both route to `QueueAction::RestoreDefaultOrder`. `entries` is now authoritative for "as queued" and `play_order` is purely the current view of it, whether that view is default, sorted, or shuffled; the current track survives every transition, as it already did across sort and shuffle. |
| 2026-08-09 | "I see something strange in playlists, it is totally different from what I see in official client." The Playlists tab listed 106,115 rows — folders, albums and individual tracks — where the official client shows 3 playlists. Cause: the `MediaTypes=Audio` filter added on 2026-08-02 to hide video/photo playlists. Measured live against the user's server: `IncludeItemTypes=Playlist&Recursive=true` returns 3 items, all `Type=Playlist`; adding `&MediaTypes=Audio` returns 106,115 items of `Type` in `{Folder, MusicAlbum, Audio}`. Emby does not intersect the two filters — supplying `MediaTypes` makes it disregard `IncludeItemTypes` and answer with everything audio-ish in the library. The 2026-08-02 fix passed its test because that test asserted the *parameter was sent*; a mock cannot tell you what a server does with a parameter, which is the general lesson here — a query-shape assertion is not a behavioural one. Reverted: `MediaTypes` is gone from `playlists::list`, and `ItemQuery::media_types` is deleted outright rather than left as a trap for the next caller (a test now asserts the key is never emitted). That restores the original 2026-08-02 complaint — non-music playlists are listed again — so it is now fixed where it actually belongs: `playlists::items` **skips** entries whose `Type` is not `Audio` instead of failing the whole batch on the first one. Opening or queueing a video playlist yields an empty list rather than an error, and, more importantly, a music playlist containing one stray non-audio row is no longer unopenable. This is a deliberate departure from the "one bad row fails the batch" convention the other listing endpoints follow: elsewhere a row that won't convert signals a real parse defect, whereas here a non-audio row is a legitimate thing for the server to return. Live check: playlist 108985 returns 14 entries, every one `Type=Audio, MediaType=Audio`. |
| 2026-08-09 | "`i` inserts tracks, not albums or artists" and "`v` selects tracks, albums, but only adds selected tracks — it doesn't work on albums" are one defect with two faces. Every container row (album, artist, genre, folder, playlist) has to be *fetched* before it can be queued, and `DataAction::TracksLoaded` carries only the tracks and their `QueueSource` — nothing about the press that asked for them. `insert_next` therefore fell through to `resolve_selection_tracks`, which keeps `MediaItem::Track` rows only, found nothing, and returned: `i` on an album was a no-op. The same filter made a visual multi-selection of albums resolve to an empty candidate list, so `v` + `a` over three albums queued nothing at all. Both need the same missing thing — state that survives the round trip. Added `AppState::pending_queue_batch: Option<QueueBatch>`: an ordered list of slots, each either already filled (a plain track, in hand) or awaiting a named `QueueSource`, plus an `Append`/`InsertNext` mode. `apply_tracks_loaded` fills the slot whose `awaiting` matches the reply's source and flushes the whole batch — in **slot order, not reply order** — once none is still waiting. That ordering is the point: N fetches come back in whatever order the server answers, and a user who selected three albums expects them queued in the order they were displayed. A single container row queued with `Enter`/`a` deliberately keeps its existing direct path (no batch, zero behaviour change); a batch is created only for `i` and for a multi-selection that actually contains a container, so an all-tracks selection is untouched. The per-row fetch is now built once in `container_fetch`, shared by both paths, so they cannot drift on which rows are queueable. At most one batch is in flight and starting another replaces it — a fetch that never replies (deduplicated away as already-in-flight, say) can delay a batch but never wedges queueing, and a reply with no batch to match simply takes the ordinary append path. `LoadTarget::QueueFetch` carries no source, so a failure cannot say which slot died: the batch is flushed with whatever arrived rather than parked forever, and the abandoned albums' `pending_album_queue_fetch` entries are dropped rather than leaked. |
| 2026-08-09 | "I can't select anything from favourites." Literally true — the cursor could not be put on a favourite at all.
